  - (d) For all but 15 or 16 of the 276 (the figure depends on the parser) the parsed file contains such an entry [counted]. These few stem from an opening whose only leaf below the vertex's sibling is a shared leaf, which the parser writes once. Nothing of them reaches `verify`, and they can be altered in the raw file alone. The parser rejects such an alteration too [read]; that was not tried. If it did not reject, that would be one input which `cp_verify` rejects and which the road through the shipped parser to `verify` accepts.
  - (e) Of an honest parsed file's 407,296 path entries 153,612, about 38 %, are never read: about 19 % of the file's bytes [counted]. 10,199 of the 153,612 contain a value that the raw proof gives somewhere [counted: once]; the others contain none (14).
  - (f) Also free: the field of the parsed proof file that contains the committed table's root [read]; nothing was run. Only its length (32 bytes) is tested (OnBytes/Maker/Carrier.lean:224; Johnson/Top/Fire/Code/FileInOf.lean:76-80). The value made from it is not handed on to `verify` (`decodeD`, Johnson/Goal/Tie/DescriptionOnBytes.lean:133-140). The field travels on inside the parsed structure, and nothing reads it there [read]; the typing does not (OnBytes/Maker/CarrierTyping.lean:272; Johnson/RunOne/Controls.lean:39-41). The root under which the table is opened is the copy's file's, so a changed bit in this field would be accepted too. This holds of this field of the proof file alone: the answers and the copy's number are read, and so is the key file's root.
  - (g) Also free [read: once]: rows at a leaf at or above the tree's size, entries without rows, and the unread path digests of stored rows and of the key file.
  - (h) In a tree that is opened once, the accepted bits of 14 (g) lie in Merkle-path digests of the parsed form, at a vertex with opened leaves beneath it [read]. The parser writes that entry; the proof file itself does not contain it. `verify`'s joint walk computes the vertex from its children and never reads the entry [read], in four runs (14 (g)) also [evaluated] (`runForest`, OnBytes/TypedOracleCompiler/Compile/Batch/Verifier.lean:428-435; `checkPrunedOpenings`, OnBytes/TypedOracleCompiler/Compile/Batch/Verifier.lean:585-600; the walk, Hash/Octopus.lean:89-109). Such a digest enters no hash chain.
  - (i) In a pruned tree the digests of the paths that the joint verification does not use are free, and only their number is tested (OnBytes/TypedOracleCompiler/Compile/Batch/Verifier.lean:119-125). A file of controls expects such an accept at one bit of the last digest of a stored row's path, where another opened leaf lies below that vertex [read] (Johnson/RunOne/Controls.lean:46-48, :56-57). That the tree runs under the pruned joint walk is [read], and nothing was run (`prJ := true` at a request's own round, `pr0 := true` at a shared one: OnBytes/Maker/LessWhole.lean:45-56, :61-73). Not tried: a request with padding, another copy, real weights.
- **Layer.** The tie between Lean and the programs: what `verify` reads of a proof against what `cp_verify` and the parser read.
- **Why it matters.** Soundness is unaffected. A false claim gains nothing beyond the bound of the soundness theorem (28) from this freedom: that theorem, and at the second stored set's policy the theorem under a policy (section 2.2), bounds its acceptance whatever the unread entries contain, because the five byte strings of the parsed form are the adversary's (Ladder.lean:401-402).
  - The soundness theorem (28) has exact match among its hypotheses. At the second stored set, where one of the accepted altered files was run (14 (g)), it is silent, and only the theorem under a policy covers that altered file (`soundUnderPolicy_of_verify_at`, Johnson/Goal/Skeleton/SoundOfVerifyPolicy.lean:63; there a false claim is an output that does not meet the files' policy at the extracted weights), with the limits of (6).
  - The theorem speaks of false claims. It says nothing on whether a proof of a true claim can be altered and pass, and soundness allows that an altered file of a true claim is accepted.
  - What has been shown to differ is the verdict on an altered file of a true claim, where `verify` is handed a parsed file that the shipped parser did not write. Whether the two disagree on the road through the shipped parser was not tried at the digests of (d). This part of the interface is tested, not proved.
- **Proved nearest to it.** `TypedOracleCompiler.Compile.Batch.runsIn_verifyB_of_strict` (OnBytes/TypedOracleCompiler/Compile/Batch/ForestSound.lean:437-449): what `verifyOneProtocol` (OnBytes/TypedOracleCompiler/Compile/Batch/Verifier.lean:672), the compiled verifier of one IOP, with the pruned walk, accepts, Lean's `verifyB` (OnBytes/TypedOracleCompiler/Compile/Batch/Verifier.lean:605), which follows whole paths, accepts with some other paths, from the same trace, where every opening's path may differ whole, not the free entries alone. Its one premise, `wq.OKG`, is that the Lean structure `wq` (which says how the rounds' messages are placed in Merkle trees, among other things) is well-formed.
  - The soundness theorem depends on this theorem [counted], through a lemma that applies it to each of the three files' typed proof strings and asks nothing of which paths those are (Johnson/TypedOracleCompiler/RecCompile/CmsAnsPr.lean:235-237) [read]. That lemma takes the premise as its own hypothesis, discharged further up from tests on numbers that are proved to hold (Johnson/Goal/Skeleton/TopAtZ.lean:182-187; `wiresOfFullD`, Johnson/Flat/Dec/WiresAtSizes.lean:118) [read]; nothing was run. Which `wq` that is, and whether it is `verify`'s own, is not known. Lean's kernel has checked the soundness theorem whichever lemmas it goes through.
  - The theorem speaks of the openings' path fields of the typed proof, not of a file's bytes. It does not say that the free entries are unread, nor that the verdict is the same at every filling of them. No lemma that says so is known.
- **Tested nearest to it.** One run in two parts, on one proof of the first stored set (examples/full-size/example.proof), at exact match, on synthetic weights. It cannot be made with the command (45).
  - The raw part [tested]: five altered raw proofs, four at digests among the 276, in both trees that are opened twice, and a fifth at a digest that `verify` does hash. `cp_verify` accepted the honest raw proof and rejected all five, and the parser rejected all five.
  - The Lean part [evaluated: known from a report alone]: six changes of one bit of the parsed form of the same proof, the claim being true. Two at entries that the walk takes (one in a tree opened once; one in a tree opened twice, the copy that is taken) were rejected, at the proof file's step alone. Four at entries that it does not take (a free entry in the tree opened once; two entries that the prover's two openings give in the tree opened twice; the copy that is not taken) were accepted. One further call ended void, by a fault of the file that drove it.
  - All four places in a tree opened twice lie in one of the two such trees. The three accepted there are the parsed file's entries for three of the four digests among the 276 that the raw part altered [read: once]. The fourth, in the other tree opened twice, was altered in the raw file alone.
  - So at these three digests of one proof, and at no more, the disagreement is shown, not only read. The altered raw proofs and the altered parsed files are two kinds of input. No one input is shown at which `cp_verify` rejects while the road from the raw proof through the shipped parser to `verify` accepts, because the parser of that run rejected these altered raw proofs too.
- **Known attack or counterexample.** None known for soundness [read]. For all accepted altered files: 14 (g).

### 67 · Lean's model is defined a little further than the shipped provers go

- **The limit.** At five kinds of intermediate value (a norm's quotient; a wide query, key or value entry; the high part of a router logit; an up projection) Lean's integer model is defined from −32,768 to 32,767 (`fits16`, IntegerModel/Model.lean:592-593). The shipped provers write these values in two signed bytes, which end at 32,639, and a stream value in five, which end below the model's 2^39 (docs/model.md, section 9; Inst/Model/Program.lean:436-440 gives the same two numbers, in a bound that no relation of this library uses). Between the two limits the model is defined, but the provers make no proof and the request gets the response `failed`.
  - (a) The protocol as Lean describes it (`theDescription`, "the IOP" below) follows the model, not the provers: no table or test of it ends at 32,639 [read in part]. The number does occur in this folder: in that unused bound, as the end of a digit that the prover chooses, as the end of the model's own clamped gate key, and in an example on the programs' worst-case test of a norm's quotient (VerifiableInference/Bounds/SomeWeightsPass.lean:164, :240). None of these makes the IOP narrower than the model.
  - (b) The reach of that reading: the IOP down to the lookups' calls, with the lookup and the layers' own rounds read line by line, with the layouts, tables and key forms, and the other kinds of step and the later chains at their heads only. No Lean was run on it, and no program was read.
  - (c) No request that reaches 32,640 to 32,767 is exhibited in this folder [counted]. The stream value's band was not searched.
  - (d) Two edges of the model's own definitions. `layerWellFormed` does not test `softmaxShift`; its range (4 to 12) is `layerSettingsInRange`'s (VerifiableInference/ModelWeights.lean). `roundShift` is meant for a shift of at least 1, and `layerWellFormed` asks that of every shift that a layer hands to it (IntegerModel/Model.lean:144-148, :651-659); at shift 0 its value is a + 1, not a [read].
- **Layer.** The tie between Lean and the programs: Lean's model and IOP against the shipped provers.
- **Why it matters.**
  - For soundness nothing is lost. The theorem's claim is about Lean's model. The IOP's intervals at these tables are the model's at both ends, so it admits nothing outside the model's domain there [read in part], and a proof in that band would prove the model's own reply; no shipped prover makes one (docs/model.md, section 9).
  - For completeness of the IOP: no table of the IOP rejects, for its range, a value inside the model's intervals that is written in signed bytes [read in part]. Beside it, the smallness test can reject honest values at some draws: 52 (k).
  - For completeness of the shipped provers: a statement at every request on which the model has a value (the third clause of `CanServe`, VerifiableInference/CanServe.lean:31-35) would fail of the shipped provers at a request in that band. The goals' completeness speaks of the IOP's own prover field, not of the shipped provers; nor does `completeAtWellFormedRequests` speak of them: it is of the honest part written in Lean (39).
  - For hiding: whether a request in that band is answered `failed` depends on an internal value, and so on the weights. This repository lists that answer among what is not hidden (docs/what-is-not-hidden.md), and a statement on zero knowledge that handed its simulator the result of Lean's model would not give it the programs' answer there. At a refused request the Lean prover returns the empty proof (`noProofForm`: a form all of whose parts are empty). The programs' service answers "failed" for every request that it does not prove, whatever the cause; a client can partly tell the causes apart (docs/limits-all-rows.tsv, row HW-532). That answer is neither the bit of `zeroKnowledgeAtEveryRequest` (served or refused) nor the label "failed" of the corollary `zeroKnowledgeWithLabel` (39), and no theorem ties them.
- **Known attack or counterexample.** None known, within the limits of the reading at (b).

### 68 · It is not proved that the guard inside `verify` never rejects an honest proof

- **The limit.** `verify` contains a guard on the blocks of the proof file's first chain. After every step but the one that receives the next token, it rejects if that step has changed the stored token. It is an auxiliary device of the soundness proof. That it never rejects an honest proof is no theorem of its own. Of the honest part written in Lean, `completeAtWellFormedRequests` bounds the chance that `verify` rejects, for whatever cause, at 2^-40 (39).
  - (a) A second text of the IOP is written in Johnson/LPFGuard/Unguarded.lean, meant as `theDescription` without the guard's test. If the reading at (b) holds, a proof that the guard rejects would be rejected without it too.
  - (b) Lean proves `verify` the same algorithm at the IOP as written in Lean and at that second text (`LPFGuard.strike_verdict`, below). That the second text is that IOP without the guard's test (constant true at two places, left out at one) is [read]. If it is, a proof is accepted with the test if and only if it is accepted without it, so that the guard's rejection is never the only cause of a rejection. This does not say that an honest proof is accepted, nor that the guard never rejects.
  - (c) That the test rejects at no state that a step returns, and so for no proof, honest or not, follows from the lemmas below [read]. No theorem states it, and `LPFGuard.strike_verdict` does not give it (Johnson/LPFGuard/AtDescription.lean:52-56). That the guard is called at three places alone is [read].
  - (d) None of this says that the guard can be taken out. The soundness proof takes from the guard's test the fact that no other step alters the stored token (`Chain.guarded_flat`, Johnson/Desc/Sound/SeatGuardLaws.lean:182; TERMS.md). It does not use the lemmas below that prove that fact of each step, so with the guard removed the proof as it stands would not go through [read].
  - (e) No theorem on the guard says that the proof file's part of the IOP is made (the function that builds it returns it rather than an error) at any public files, so that both texts may reject everything (52 (a)). It is made at the written copy, at the format's own second hash function (`AtFullSide.Top.theProofLaid_zFull_returns`, AtFullSide/Top/LiveAtFull.lean:54, in the side library), and at that copy with only the policy changed, where the changed files are in scope (`seats_zGap`, `seats_zAt`, AtFullSide/Top/GatesOpen.lean:117, :123); of other files nothing is proved. That the part is made does not say that any proof is accepted.
  - (f) Each text has its own proof parser, on the proof in parsed form. `LPFGuard.strike_verdict` says nothing about a parser of raw bytes or of any other byte format (none is in this folder), nothing of a parser that a run hands to `verify`, nothing of code as it runs, interpreted or natively compiled, and nothing of the programs, `cp_verify` among them.
  - (g) In the second text one field, which holds the guard's test (`PureSeat.same`), is made constant true; that the guard's calls at both of the two places take their test from it is [read: four definitions]. Further limits: the opening comments of Johnson/LPFGuard/AtDescription.lean and Johnson/LPFGuard/Unguarded.lean.
  - (h) `cp_verify` makes no such test: in its source the received token is stored by one line, in one place, out of the other steps' reach [read].
- **Layer.** The protocol as written in Lean; (h) is of the tie between Lean and the programs.
- **Why it matters.** It affects no statement on soundness: the soundness theorem (28) is proved of `verify` exactly as it stands, with the guard at all three places. What it affects is whether an honest proof could be rejected.
- **Proved nearest to it.**
  - `LPFGuard.strike_verdict` (Johnson/LPFGuard/StrikeAt.lean:54; statement: Johnson/LPFGuard/AtDescription.lean:98): `verify`, given the IOP as written in Lean or the second text, is the same algorithm at every input.
  - `LPFGuard.strike_description` (Johnson/LPFGuard/StrikeAt.lean:40; statement: Johnson/LPFGuard/AtDescription.lean:95): the two texts are equal as objects, so no statement of Lean can tell them apart; the equation at `verify` follows from that alone.
  - `LPFGuard.strike` (Johnson/LPFGuard/Strike.lean:108; statement: Johnson/LPFGuard/Unguarded.lean:364): what `verify` takes from the proof file's part of the IOP (the data on its rounds and its decision) is the same for both texts, at every function in the second hash function's place and at all public files, with no premise.
  - `LPFGuard.strike_desc` (Johnson/LPFGuard/Strike.lean:100; statement: Johnson/LPFGuard/Unguarded.lean:355), with a lemma beside it: wherever the proof file's part is made, the first chain's list of steps with the guard's test equals, as functions and with their rejection messages, the list with a test that always accepts.
  - The lemmas of (c): every step of the first chain but the decision that receives the token leaves the stored token unchanged whenever it returns a state (`seatBut_reply`, Johnson/LPFGuard/Makers.lean:293; `thePure_but`, Johnson/LPFGuard/Lots.lean:271); `LPFGuard.guarded_reply` (Johnson/LPFGuard/Strike.lean:62-65). The test is applied only after a step that returns a state (Johnson/Desc/SeatGuard.lean:64-70).
  - The three places: the driver (`firstChain`, Johnson/Desc/First/Driver/Chain.lean:254-255), but for the decision at the one place that receives the token; what is joined after a block of the first chain (Johnson/Desc/ProofFile/Frames1.lean:170-175); the first half of the block at "H:1" (`h1Own`, Johnson/Desc/ProofFile/Lots/H1.lean:31-37). `Block.guarded` (Johnson/Desc/SeatGuard.lean:63-70) runs the block's decision and then `seatSame` (Johnson/Desc/SeatGuard.lean:58-61). That file's opening comment says that a guard can reject an honest file (Johnson/Desc/SeatGuard.lean:24): it speaks of a guard in general and names no IOP.
- **Tested nearest to it.** The guard rejected no honest proof in the runs [evaluated] (14).
- **Known attack or counterexample.** None known for soundness.

### 4, 9, 11 · Outside the scope of the Lean work

- **The limit.** The numbers name this one entry.
  - (a) Outside the scope of the Lean work are: anything of `cp_verify`, `cp_commit` or `cp_prove`; anything of `verify-on-bytes` as a verifier (13, 3); anything of a natively compiled Lean program (but the comment on `verify` counts a run for the final statement only if it runs that very term, which no run can: 65); that Lean's integer model is what the programs compute, and how far it lies from the floating-point model.
  - (b) Where an honest committer or prover is needed (completeness, correctness of extraction, binding of the commitment in the opening form, no two openings, hiding), the C++ programs are assumed to follow the honest side of the protocol.
  - (c) Lean has no committer's or prover's side of the protocol as the programs run it: its honest side is written as definitions (below; 10, 14, 15).
- **Layer.** The programs, and the tie between Lean and the programs.
- **Proved nearest to it.** The protocol's three honest provers and its honest committer are written in Lean, as definitions and not as programs (39). No theorem ties them to a program.

### 76 · The tests with lying proofs are few in kind

- **The limit.** No lying proof that was put to the command `lean-verify` or to the executable form was accepted (at three the command gave no verdict), but they are few in kind, and none comes from a prover built to search for a false proof that passes. They are of two kinds: the first made for a false reply, the second claiming the true reply and carrying a lie inside the proof.
  - (a) All that were put to the command `lean-verify` are at exact match, at one commitment, on synthetic weights.
  - (b) Every rejection of a lying proof occurred at one step of the verifier, the one that reads the proof file. Inside that step all the proofs that were made for a false reply were rejected at one and the same place, the base case of the first chain [evaluated].
  - (c) No run shows the policy's own test rejecting anything (how far any test is the policy's own: "Beyond a tolerant policy's limits", below).
  - (d) No proof of a false claim that carries the second kind of lie has been put to the verifier.
  - (e) No lying proof under a tolerant policy has been put to the command `lean-verify`.
  - (f) Nothing in the files shows which program made them, or that the proofs of the second kind lie inside the proof.
- **Layer.** The tie between Lean and the programs.
- **Why it matters.** It affects no statement on soundness: that is the theorem's (28), not these tests'. A reader should not take these rejections for more than the cases tried.
- **Tested nearest to it.** Each verdict is [evaluated].
  - *What they were.* In the reference run, at full size, 57 lying proofs were put to the command `lean-verify`, of 3 requests. The first kind, 24, were made for a false reply, 21 false replies to one request: what is false in each is the one next token. The second kind, 33, claim the true replies to three requests and carry a lie inside the proof. None was accepted: 54 were rejected, all 24 of the first kind and 30 of the second, and at three there was no verdict. `cp_verify`, in runs of its own on the same files, accepted none of them either: it ended each of the 57 with status 1 and a line that says no, the same three among them [tested]. Every one of these files had been judged before, and the outcomes expected of the run were those earlier outcomes (14 (d)). By the 30 rejections of the second kind, not every proof of a true claim that stands as its prover wrote it is accepted: what (14) says of accepts is said of proofs made by an honest run of the prover.
  - *No verdict.* In the reference run, at 3 of the second kind the command gave no verdict, because its parser refuses their files before Lean is called. The Lean verifier neither accepted nor rejected these, so they do not count as rejections.
  - *Where they fell.* The figures of this point are of other runs. Inside the step that reads the proof file, the 24 of the first kind and ten of the second were rejected at one and the same place, the base case of the first chain (one run; the place is printed by a file that is not in this repository) [evaluated]. Twenty-two further proofs of the second kind, of two requests, at one commitment, lie in another way than those ten. Twenty of them were rejected at a later check than the other 34 rejected proofs; this rests on one evaluation of each proof and on the order of the checks [read]. The two groups differ in other ways too: the shape of the request, the programs that made them, and the text of the command's files at the evaluation. So it is not known whether that comes from the kind of lie. The other two are files that the parser refuses; at both a rejection had been expected.
  - *Beyond a tolerant policy's limits.* Apart from the command, the executable form rejected, at the same step, two full-size proofs made for a claim that is false by a tolerant policy, by their label one beyond the limit on the count alone and one beyond the limit on the mean alone (one run). By their label the prover that made them altered no value and only went on past its own test on the policy. A third file from the same program, set to alter nothing, was accepted. The rejection names a place, not the policy: no test on the proof's values is the policy's own, because the verifier is not shown the costs (the format's hidden numbers: docs/format/11-4-output-head.md). That the rejection is owed to the limits rests on the comparison with that third file, and on that no value was altered, which nothing in the files shows. The second false file has no such counterpart, a file within the limits at its own window (the window: the 1,024 tokens that the model's run reads: the prompt, the output, then padding).
  - *Where they came from.* Those of the first kind in the reference run, 24, come from a test version of the prover that lies through a test switch which the sources in this repository do not contain, run with an option that skips the prover's own check before it releases a proof. The second kind came labelled as the work of cheating versions of the prover: eleven of another version of that same test program, twenty-two of another cheating prover. Of these labels (f) holds.
  - *Negative tests.* The five that the command rejected in the reference run are of a shallow kind, an honest proof beside a claim that is not its own, with no proof file altered: three of the proofs that came labelled as the prover's, each beside another next token; the stored full-size proof beside another last token of its 1,001; and the same stored proof beside another commitment ID, which was rejected at the commitment ID's test, in front of the three files' steps. Each of these proofs, beside its own claim, was accepted in the same run. They show that at these proofs another claimed token, and at one of them another commitment ID, is rejected, and they say nothing on a cheating prover.
  - The cheating programs and these files are not in this repository, so these trials cannot be repeated from it.
- **Known attack or counterexample.** None known.

## 2. The soundness theorem

THEOREM.md states the theorem in plain words and says how to check it. This section contains what limits it: first entry 28, then sections 2.2 to 2.5.

- **Proved, of `verify`.** Soundness at exact match, in the random-oracle model: `sound_of_verify_at` (28). Wider forms, for every proof parser within a bound and for every policy in scope: section 2.2.
- **Proved of illustrative systems, not of the Lean verifier's own system, whose committer is a constant one (55).** `SoundAndBinding`, `SoundOpened`, G9 and G9bits (55). An illustrative system keeps `verify` and has a committer written in Lean. The first two are proved of the compiled system at the honest part too (55 (b); 39).
- **Not covered.** The thirteen points of section 2.3. The C++ programs are outside every statement here (section 1).
- **What it rests on.** Section 2.4.
- **Known attack.** None known (28).

### 28 · The statement of the soundness theorem, and the definitions beneath it

- **Proved.** Johnson/Goal/Skeleton/SoundOfVerifyAt.lean:69-73, :78 (namespace `VerifierAndGoals.AtTheName`). Lean's kernel checks the proofs. They rest on no axiom beyond Lean's three standard ones (`propext`, `Classical.choice`, `Quot.sound`) and on no conjecture on error-correcting codes (18). The random oracle and the second hash function belong to the statement, not to the proofs' axioms.

  ```lean
  theorem sound_of_verify_at (Z : VerifierAndGoals.PublicFiles) (pp : Inst.Model.ScalesAndPolicy)
      (scope : InScope Z = true) (read : readFiles Z = .ok pp)
      (exact : pp.pol = Inst.Model.AcceptancePolicy.exact) :
      VerifiableInference.Sound (systemOfRecord (atSecondHashFunction theDescription) Z (settingsOfRead pp))
        fun t => VerifierAndGoals.soundnessErrorInQueries t 1

  theorem nearestSound_of_verify : NearestSound theDescription fun t => VerifierAndGoals.soundnessErrorInQueries t 1
  ```

  - `Z`: the three public files. `readFiles Z = .ok pp`: Lean's reader parses them without error and returns the parameters `pp`. `settingsOfRead pp`: the model's public settings as read.
  - `theDescription`: the protocol as Lean describes it. `atSecondHashFunction`: the slot of the second hash function, filled with the format's concrete function (60).
  - `systemOfRecord …`: the Lean verifier's system. Its verifier field is `verify` (Ladder.lean:416, :436).
  - `AcceptancePolicy.exact`: the policy (256, 256, 0) (Inst/Model/ProgramR.lean:286-287).
  - `Sound` (VerifiableInference/Statements.lean:76-99): there is a straightline extractor, which at given public files sees the commitment and the committer's hash queries and nothing of any proof (in the theorem: one such extractor at each set of public files: 69). For every committer who queries the hash function at most t₁ times and every prover who queries it at most t₂ times, where t = t₁ + t₂, the prover picking the prompt, the output and the proof, the probability (over the hash function and the adversary's coins) that the verifier accepts an output that is not the model's at the extracted weights is at most the error at t.
    - Two who share one budget of t queries as they go are covered at 2 t at most (LIMITS.md, L38).
    - Where the extractor gives no weights, every accept counts as a failure (VerifiableInference/Statements.lean:81-82).
  - `VerifierAndGoals.soundnessErrorInQueries t n` = 16 (t + n + 2^23) / 2^128 + (t + n + 2^23 + 2)^2 / 2^256 (Ladder/Bits.lean:96-98; 36). After q hash queries, the committer's and the prover's added up, that is about q/2^124 + 2^-101: never smaller than about 2^-101, and about 2^-60 at q = 2^64.
  - `NearestSound` (Ladder/Package.lean:100-103) is the goals' own form: the same at every such `Z`. The three hypotheses are inside that definition.
  - The general notion. In the terms of the definition that this library follows (ZKCP/Defs.lean; CREDITS.md) `Sound` is index-binding knowledge soundness in its witness-free case (the terms, "Soundness"). For every argument system `sound_iff` (VerifiableInference/Agreement.lean:232-235) says that `Sound` and `HasIndexBinding` (ZKCP/Defs.lean) are equivalent. That `HasIndexBinding` matches the definition that it follows is [read]; no theorem can say that. At `verify` the theorem is stated in that form too (`indexBinding_of_verify_at`, Johnson/Goal/Skeleton/SoundOfVerifyForms.lean:341), and a second theorem names the extractor, the IOP's own (`indexBindingWith_of_verify_at`, Johnson/Goal/Skeleton/SoundOfVerifyForms.lean:417). What these forms do not give: (69), (70), (71). See also THEOREM.md, "What the commitment gives, as proved".
  - `Sound` differs from the general notion in six points:
    - No witness part, since the relation has no witness: an output either is the model's or is not. No form with a witness (the model's whole run, read off the proof and the prover's queries) is stated at `verify`. The proof file's lemma has an extractor; the key file's and the copy's file's are soundness statements without one (Johnson/Top/InnerCompiledVerifier.lean:157-173). Beneath them the form of a sub-protocol's own lemma has an extractor (`VerifiableInference.Desc.Sound.Piece.GoodIn`, Johnson/Desc/Sound/GoodIn.lean), but the chains of the proof file are then composed in a plain form, with no witness and no extractor (Johnson/Desc/Plain/Leads.lean:5) [read].
    - An error in one argument, not two: the committer's and the prover's query budgets enter as their sum.
    - Public files that are fixed before the hash function is sampled (a provider who tunes them with the hash function in hand is outside the statement: 5), and that are outside the key (69).
    - Exact match (44).
    - Files in scope (35).
    - One hash function that is not the oracle (60).
  - Three hypotheses, each a closed test on the public files. The files are in scope, which `verify` tests: one fixed proof format and model size, where only the scale file and eight numbers of the parameter file may vary (35). The files are read, which follows from the scope. The policy is exact match, which `verify` does not test (44). All three hold at the written copy of the stored set (`VerifierAndGoals.storedPublicFiles`; `VerifierAndGoals.filesRead`, Johnson/Goal/Files/FilesReadProof.lean:48; 73 (k)).
  - No lemma is assumed, at a price. Sixteen conditions on the public files that the proofs need, and that no theorem gives for all files in scope, are tests that `verify` runs itself: fourteen in Johnson/Desc/Laid/ProofAsks.lean:9-12 and two on the key file's side (25, 48, 43). Soundness does not need them proved; completeness does.
    - They are part of the gate (`protocolDataWellFormed`, Johnson/Goal/Description/Live.lean:52-78): the plan must be made; the key file's part and the proof file's part must be built, each with the IOP's own tests and then these sixteen; two depths, a test on numbers and six non-empty lists must fit. Where any part of the gate rejects, `verify` rejects everything (52).
    - A guard on the verifier's own state runs too, and the proof relies on it (68).
- **The limit.** A fault would most likely hide in a definition inside the statement, not in a proof; in tactics and elaborators of this library's own that run at build time (18); or in the files on coding theory (12). No check by a machine says that a definition means what its name promises. What the theorem does not say: section 2.3. What it rests on, with the points 28 (a) to 28 (d): section 2.4.
- **Layer.** The protocol as written in Lean.
- **Why it matters.** What is proved is what these definitions say, of the Lean verifier `verify`, and not of the C++ program that users run (45, 56). The name of the notion does not mean that a prover knew the weights (64), nor that a commitment stands for the same weights under all public files (69). The theorem bounds one proof at a time (70).
- **Known attack or counterexample.** None known: nothing found makes the theorem false or weaker than its words [read]. What limits its meaning is listed in section 2.5.

### 2.2 Wider forms of the theorem, and the numbered goals

Two files beside the theorem of (28) go further, with the same theorems beneath.

- **Every proof parser within n queries.** `sound_of_verify_parsers_at` (Johnson/Goal/Skeleton/SoundOfVerifyMore.lean:75-86): the same three hypotheses; `Sound` for every format of proof, every honest prover's algorithm and every proof parser that queries the hash function at most n times on every input, at `VerifierAndGoals.soundnessErrorInQueries t n`. The error grows with n. A proof parser does not see the commitment and cannot reject (Ladder.lean:394-398).
- **Every policy in scope.** `soundUnderPolicy_of_verify_at`, `soundUnderPolicyAt_of_verify` (Johnson/Goal/Skeleton/SoundOfVerifyPolicy.lean:63-72): without the hypothesis of exact match, at every set of public files in scope, for every format, prover's algorithm and proof parser within n queries, at the IOP's own extractor and at `VerifierAndGoals.soundnessErrorInQueries t n`, nobody gets an output accepted that does not meet the files' own policy at the extracted weights (the goals' `SoundUnderPolicyAt`, Ladder.lean:550-561). What this form does not say: (6).
- **One numbered goal with a level.** `g8bits_of_verify : G8bits theDescription` (Johnson/Goal/Skeleton/SoundOfVerifyPolicy.lean:75-76). It adds a bound on a count of the verifier's own queries, in mixed units (`VerifierWithin`, Ladder/Bits.lean:128-132: part are counted as queries to the hash function, the compiled verifier's as the compiler's typed queries, Ladder/Bits.lean:116-121). No goal bounds the number of strings that the verifier hashes (36). By the goals' own ties (Ladder/BitsTies.lean:25, :71, :79) G7bits, G7 and G8 each follow from it by one term, of one tie or two. G7bits has a named theorem (`g7bits_of_verify`, Johnson/Goal/Skeleton/SoundOfVerifyGoals.lean:77); G7 and G8 have none.

**The numbered goals** are of this project's own making and are not among the six top statements (section 2.3). Four have a level (Ladder/Bits.lean:20-24):

- G7bits: soundness at exact match at the error function `soundnessErrorInQueries`.
- G7'bits: the same, whatever function stands in the place of the second hash function.
- G8bits: soundness under the policy, at the extractor of the IOP, at `soundnessErrorInQueries`.
- G9bits: the commitment binds to the weights that the extractor gives, at `bindingErrorInQueries`.

G9 and G9bits are binding of the commitment, in the opening form (`BindsWith`, VerifiableInference/MoreStatements.lean:188-202), at the IOP's own extractor, with the honest committer kept within its budget (`BindsAt`, Ladder.lean:587-590). G9 gives correctness of extraction at each fixed choice of the committer's coins (`BindsWith.atRandomness`, VerifiableInference/BindsAtRandomness.lean:44-51; what that says of a program's commitment: 64). Ladder.lean has their forms at an error of one quarter (G7, G7', G8, G9) and one goal on completeness, under a policy, asked at the two named sets of files alone (G3'pol). `sound_of_verify_at` itself is none of them. It has the form `NearestSound`, which, unlike the goals of Ladder.lean, puts no budget on the honest side: that it holds says less than that these goals hold (Ladder/Package.lean:27-32).

**The status of these theorems.** The full check builds the theorems named in the three points above, with `g7bits_of_verify`, from nothing, and each rests on no axiom beyond Lean's three standard ones: each is among the names that its audit looks up [counted], and the audit's verdict on axioms covers every declaration of the library (18) [tested]. Asked of Lean as terms, in one run, the three one-term consequences rested on no axiom beyond these three [evaluated]; for G7 and G8 no file of this repository contains the term. Each file's own proof is a few applications of other theorems. These statements have been read less closely than that of `sound_of_verify_at`: the search for ways in which a theorem could be true for the wrong reason went at that theorem alone [read in part].

**Five limits hold of all these theorems:**

1. "Proved" means Lean's kernel on three axioms (18).
2. The verifier is `verify` with its gate, whose accept of an honest proof is proved in the random-oracle model, at exact match alone, for the honest part written in Lean and up to a stated error, and at the real hash function only evaluated (52).
3. The level is 124 bits a query, the files are fixed before the hash function is sampled, and the second hash function is a fixed function: G7' does not follow.
4. Under a policy other than exact match, "meets the policy" is this library's own definition, not the published test (Token-DiFR's) whose form its clause (a) has: to meet clause (a), stated in "Terms used on this page", is not to pass that test (Policy/Front/MeetsPolicy.lean:147-151). Policies with C = theta leave up to cap counted tokens free.
5. Coding theory lies beneath (12).

**Not proved of the Lean verifier's system:** G7' and G7'bits (60); G9 and G9bits (binding of the commitment, in the opening form: 55); G3'pol (completeness: 52). G9 and G9bits are proved of illustrative systems alone, and so is the pair G8bits with G9bits at one extractor (55, row `theWideDigits`).

### 2.3 What the theorem says nothing on

1. Any policy but exact match (44); its wider form: section 2.2.
2. What "the model" is: the committed integer weights (those that the extractor finds: 64), run at public files that the provider chooses (54), read off the committed matrix as Lean reads them (59).
3. Public files chosen after the hash function is sampled (5).
4. The second hash function as anything but one fixed function (60, 7).
5. Completeness: where the gate rejects, `verify` rejects everything and the statement holds for that reason (52 (a)).
6. A verifier that behaves otherwise at the real hash function alone (51).
7. Five of the six top statements, all but `Sound` (the six: `Sound`, `SoundAndBinding`, `SoundOpened`, `Complete`, `ZeroKnowledge`, `CompleteSoundZeroKnowledge`: VerifiableInference/Statements.lean; 39, 55). `Complete` as written cannot hold of any IOP that meets the goals of Ladder.lean (39); its form on the requests that can be served is proved of the compiled system at the honest part, not of the Lean verifier's own system (39, 52). `ZeroKnowledge` as written is proved of no system. In another form, with one bit for each request told to the simulator, zero knowledge is proved of the compiled system at the honest part (39); that form is not `ZeroKnowledge`. At `theDescription` one conditional theorem gives index privacy after hashing from 20 conditions, for a scheme whose key is the whole public key (15, 75; 15 (8)).
8. Other sizes: the full size alone, which is a family: the full-size layout, eight free numbers, any lawful scale file (35).
9. The programs (45, 56). What comes from the user must reach `verify` from the user (37).
10. "128 bits": the soundness theorem's conclusion is an error of about q/2^124 + 2^-101 (`VerifierAndGoals.soundnessErrorInQueries t 1`: 28; the line `sound` calls it `finalSoundnessError`, Johnson/Face/FinalStatement.lean:225), not 2^-128. Beneath it three theorems bound the error of every round by 1/2^128. At the rounds that sample query positions, 20 to 32 of those 128 bits are the proof of work, by the parameter file's own numbers (36 (f)) [counted].
11. Whether a proof of a true claim can be altered and still pass (non-malleability): in the parsed form it can [evaluated] (14, 66).
12. One commitment ID under two sets of public files (69).
13. More than one proof under one commitment ID: a union bound alone (70).

### 2.4 What the theorem rests on

The proof of `sound_of_verify_at` in its own file (Johnson/Goal/Skeleton/SoundOfVerifyAt.lean:74) is two applications of other theorems. Beneath them are:

- The laws of the thirteen kinds of sub-protocol on the proof file's first chain and of the 77 connecting rows between them, each a theorem derived from what the gate establishes about the public files.
- The soundness of the offline IOP (the key file's), round by round and for one stated relation (`keySound_holds`, Johnson/Goal/Conditions/KeyOpenHolds.lean:87; at the gate's accept `keySound_at_live_holds`, Johnson/Goal/Conditions/KeyOpenHolds.lean:95).
- The soundness of the link IOP (the copy's file's) (`entrySound`, Johnson/Goal/Conditions/EntrySits.lean:52; statement: Johnson/Top/InnerCompiledVerifier.lean:160-166).
- The laws of the shared chain, for a batch of as many requests as the scope allows, and of the last chain.
- Three statements that join the chains, so that the steps of the other chains and of the key file and the copy's file are covered by further lemmas, and none is assumed.
- Fifteen further closed tests on the plan, with the condition that the fourteenth entry test brings (48), which follow from the gate's accept.

**Five counts, so that no two are taken for one.** Thirteen kinds of sub-protocol occur on the first chain; with one law for the connecting rows they make fourteen laws with content. The Lean structure that gathers them has sixteen fields: the two further fields, for two kinds that occur on the other chains and files, hold vacuously, because no such step occurs on the first chain. Sixteen conditions on the public files are tests of the verifier (28). `innerCompiledVerifier_sound` has sixteen named hypotheses (13, 3). The three counts of sixteen are unrelated.

**28 (a) · The form of the theorem on `innerCompiledVerifier` with digests as answers needs a text with no hard-coded hash answer.**

- **The limit.** The form of the theorem on `innerCompiledVerifier` with digests as answers, which the proof goes through, says something of a sampled hash function only if the protocol's text and its wiring to the files contain no hard-coded answer of that function. This is no hypothesis of the theorem and cannot be one: it can only be seen at the text (Johnson/Top/InnerCompiledVerifierByPublicReading.lean:102-103, :110-112). That it holds of `theDescription`, but for one written value that nothing reads ("What the reading found"), is [read]; it rests on the readings of (5) and 51 (b). An answer could enter only through the public files themselves, and a provider who tunes them with the real hash function in hand is not covered (5).
- **Why it matters.** A text that queries the hash function and discards the answers, with true digests written in as constants, meets every hypothesis that a true text meets and passes every acceptance test [paper] (Johnson/Top/InnerCompiledVerifierByPublicReading.lean:100-102).
- **Proved nearest to it.** The coding of the hash queries depends on answers of the hash function, which does not fit the basic random-oracle step. So the proof goes through the form of the theorem on `innerCompiledVerifier` with digests as answers (Johnson/Top/InnerCompiledVerifierByPublicReading.lean), under six further conditions, which are proved at `theDescription`. It does not go through `innerCompiledVerifier_sound` (13, 3). A second theorem has the same statement and is proved through soundness as an IOP (`sound_of_verify_at_through_the_IOP`, E2E/SoundAsIOPAtFilesProofs.lean): what it adds, and what not: LIMITS.md, L89.
- **What the reading found.** All of this field: [read]. At `theDescription` no hard-coded answer is read: one is written beneath `verify`, and nothing reads it (the third point below; 51).
  - The digest in each chain's given data is a fixed value, 32 bytes of 0x01, written without the hash function and made from no answer of it (`fixedConfigurationDigest`, Johnson/Desc/TheCfg.lean:20-21, used at Johnson/Goal/Description/Plan.lean:39-41).
  - The real digest of the public files and the seven digests of the chains' parameters reach `verify` only as the random oracle's own answers (Ladder.lean:322-326, :404-411). The digest of the three public files is an answer of the hash function (`readConfig`): it depends on the hash function that is sampled.
  - The written value of (51) reaches nothing that is compared or hashed.
  - The opening comment of Johnson/Top/InnerCompiledVerifierByPublicReading.lean says that the library's IOPs at a plan's chains contain the public files' digest in each chain's given data, so that none is covered [paper] (Johnson/Top/InnerCompiledVerifierByPublicReading.lean:113-115). It excepts no IOP; the first point above is why it does not hold of `theDescription`.
- **A further limit.** That this fixed value's parameter bytes are overwritten before any string is hashed is [counted] [paper], not a theorem (Johnson/Goal/Description/Plan.lean:14-20). That is a matter of completeness, not of this condition.

**28 (b) · One hash function.**

- **The limit.** At least two assumptions stay outside Lean: that the format's real hash function behaves like one random function, and that the second hash function, a fixed one, does not interact with it (7, 60).
- **Proved nearest to it.** The goals' model samples one random function on byte strings. Inside the proof the protocol is analysed with nine typed, independent random functions. A Lean theorem passes between the two (`Rom.binding_overOne`, Rom/ExitBind.lean:117, with `Rom.U_map_readBlocks`, Rom/Prefix.lean:88: a coding that is one to one and onto, at the same error; in that name "binding" is index binding: 10). On the way the count of queries grows from t to t + (8 + n), for the verifier's public readings and the proof parser.

**28 (c) · Where the error bound holds.** Each law holds at its declared error. One theorem puts the compiler's own bound, at a round's error of at most 2/2^128 and at most 2^23 - 9 typed queries of the compiled verifier, under `soundnessErrorInQueries`, and only where `soundnessErrorInQueries` is below 1 (`errorAtBits`, Johnson/Goal/Conditions/RowsAtTheRecord.lean:187). At all t and n it would be false (Johnson/Goal/Conditions/RowsAtTheRecord.lean:18-19).

**28 (d) · Coding theory.** Mutual correlated agreement (MCA) for Reed–Solomon codes up to (just inside) the Johnson radius, and the step from two words to many rows, are theorems of this library as Lean states them. That they follow from the published theorems is [read] (12).

See also (18), on what runs at build time and on notations, macros and instances, and (27), on where the laws' conditions are shown to hold.

### 2.5 What limits the meaning of the theorem

The gaps that limit what the theorem means are 25, 44, 51, 52, 54, 55, 56, 58, 60, 64 and 77. Where a fault would most likely hide: (28), "The limit".

**How much of what lies beneath the statement was read.** The statement unfolded to 894 modules. About 150 of them are named in a reading [read in part: about 150 of 894, each once, in minutes, with no Lean run]. The other 740 or so were only swept by program for one kind of fault [counted].

**Comments.** The files that state the goals are pinned by checksum, comments too (50). Where a comment and Lean's printed statement differ, the statement counts. Comments that say less than is proved:

- The comments named in (58).

Comments that can be read for more than is proved: two phrases in the comments of Johnson/PVR/Program.lean; which two is on no record [read].

## 3. Gaps at the theorem and its verifier

Its entries are in sections 3.1 to 3.3.

### 3.1 What the statement means

Its entries: 44 · 54 · 55 · 64 · 77 · 72 · 78 · 36 · 5 · 69 · 70 · 71 · 60 · 7 · 51 · 35 · 50 · 42 · 80.

### 44 · `verify` never tests that the policy is exact match

- **The limit.** Exact match, (C, theta, cap) = (256, 256, 0), is one of the theorem's three hypotheses, and `verify` does not test it. The scope test's own clause on the policy asks only 2^8 ≤ theta; the reader, whose accept it asks too, refuses a policy without C < 2^32, theta ≤ C and cap ≤ 1,024 (Ladder.lean:266-269, :307-312): C, theta and cap are three of the eight free numbers of the scope. Policies that leave every counted token free stay in scope: (256, 256, 1) at one counted token, and theta = C with cap = 1,024 at every request (Ladder.lean:304-306).
- **Layer.** The protocol as written in Lean: a hypothesis of the statement that the Lean verifier does not test.
- **Why it matters.** An accept at another policy in scope looks the same as one at exact match. Of such an accept the theorem says nothing, and its wider form says only that the output meets that policy (section 2.2). So a user, or the program that calls `verify`, must check the three policy lines of the parameter file against (256, 256, 0). A commitment ID is of one parameter file, and so of one policy, short of a hash collision [read] (69): the same weights need another key file under another policy.
- **Proved nearest to it.** What another policy allows (`Policy.meetsPolicy_iff_budget`, Policy/Front/MeetsPolicyBounds.lean:470): an output meets the policy exactly if the model has scores for it, a token after a full window is the model's next token, and one can set aside at most cap counted tokens, of whose gaps nothing is asked, and charge each C − 256, so that the unclipped gaps of all the others (each at most C − 256) plus these charges sum to at most n (theta − 256), n being the number of counted tokens.
  - At the second stored set's numbers, (25856, 512, 1): under 100 counted tokens no token reaches the clip and the mean gap is at most 256 integer units (`Policy.gaps_of_meetsPolicy_qExample_short`, Policy/Front/MeetsPolicyAtGap.lean:218). From 100 on one token may be anything, if the others are the model's next tokens (`Policy.meetsPolicy_qExample_of_one_free`, Policy/Front/MeetsPolicyAtGap.lean:298).
  - The written copy of the stored full-size set (`VerifierAndGoals.storedPublicFiles`) has exact match, and the written copy of the other named set (`VerifierAndGoals.zGap`) has the policy (25856, 512, 1) (`VerifierAndGoals.filesRead`, Johnson/Goal/Files/FilesReadProof.lean:48; `FilesRead`, Ladder.lean:604-608). No theorem ties these copies to the files in examples/ (73 (k)).

### 54 · "The model" is the committed integer weights, at public files that the provider writes

- **The limit.** "The model" in a false claim about the committed model's next token is the committed integer weights, run by the library's integer program at the public scale file and parameter file. They are the weights that the extractor finds, which need not be the ones that the committer used (64).
  - (a) It is not the floating-point model, and no theorem says how close the two are.
  - (b) No theorem shows that a real model's weights meet the program's range conditions.
  - (c) Prompts have 1 to 1,024 tokens, out of a vocabulary of 202,048. Any prefix of an output counts. There is no stop rule. Ties between tokens go to the lowest index. Where the extractor gives weights, an accepted empty output never counts as a forgery under `Sound` (the policy's own definition, at exact match, does ask for a non-empty output).
  - (d) The quantization parameters (the scale file's 696 words and five more numbers) are public and are the provider's choice. They are no part of what the statements call the weights (VerifiableInference/ModelWeights.lean:10), so no statement on hiding covers them. In the terms of the definition that this library follows (CREDITS.md) they are public parameters of the index: in the goals' statements the public files are fixed before the weights and are the same in both experiments (Ladder/Package.lean:173-174), and the simulator is chosen after them. What they tell of the weights is counted by no statement on hiding. A fitted model's quantization parameters are made from its weights, and so tell something of these (VerifiableInference/ModelWeights.lean:178-180; docs/what-is-not-hidden.md, item 5).
  - (e) A layer's thirteen words are free layer by layer, so a scale file in scope can switch off any chosen subset of the 96 attention and feed-forward blocks, up to all of them, whatever the weights [paper]. The validity tests fix sizes and ranges, not the model: all-zero matrices pass, and some valid settings make every layer add nothing [paper] (VerifiableInference/ModelWeights.lean:169-171).
  - (f) The scale file's digest, with the other two public files' digests, is hashed into the commitment ID, so `verify` accepts a given commitment ID with one scale file only, short of a hash collision [read]. No Lean theorem states it, and the soundness statement takes the files as fixed beforehand. It does not show that the scales are sensible. No goal ties a commitment to one set of public files (69).
  - (g) "The model" also contains five tables (cos, sin, exp, sigmoid, SiLU) that are constants of the Lean text (VerifiableInference/ModelWeights.lean:171-172). No theorem speaks of their content, and none ties them to the parameter file's digests. They are not the four lookup tables of section 1.1.
- **Layer.** The protocol as written in Lean: what the statement means.
- **Why it matters.** The model runs at public scale and parameter files that the provider writes, and a scale file in scope can switch off any of the model's blocks. So a user must hold and read the public files, or compare the scale file with the stored example's, just as it must hold the right commitment. That no real model's weights are shown to fit the integer program narrows what can be certified; it does not weaken soundness. What this entry tells is by design and is told in the files that state the goals; it is easy to overlook.
- **Tested nearest to it.**
  - On (g): on the decoded tables each entry lies within 0.5 of the real function [read]. No test in this repository says so.
  - On (b): a count outside Lean, by the integer-model package's own range checks, found none failing on each of some hundreds of windows of 1,024 tokens at a public model's 4-bit weights, a third party's conversion to GGUF [counted]. A window is a prompt of 524 tokens cut from WikiText-2, a stored or altered reply, then token 0. That count does not show the following (LIMITS.md, L27):
    - The windows come from far fewer prompts than there are windows: they are not independent texts. About a dozen further passes are not reported.
    - The count is the package's arithmetic, not Lean's evaluation, and that the package which ran has the range checks of the package in this repository is not shown. Lean has seven tests for its eight range conditions; the eighth has none of its own. That the package's intervals are Lean's or narrower in all seven, and the window's padding Lean's, is [read] against a written account of what `CanServe` asks, not against the Lean files. That the two compute the same values rests on both following docs/model.md; the two were not compared.
    - Not tested: that the weights lie in `ModelWeights settings` (the package's loader checks bounds on every entry that are Lean's or narrower [read]; the arrays' sizes were not tested, and the conversion's five files were checked by size, not by checksum); `settingsAreValid` at that parameter file; the model's tables, entry for entry; and whether the replies are the integer model's own, while completeness speaks only of output tokens that are the model's.
    - Not known: whether Lean's reader takes that parameter file, whether it is in the soundness theorem's scope, or whether it is at exact match. It is not in this repository; no Lean program was given it; its lines C, theta and cap are on no record. It was a copy with one line added, `temp 256`, which the forward pass does not read [read]. Its table of scales is the file whose SHA-256 examples/maverick/expected.txt gives for config/KL.bin, by one comparison; its `policy.txt` was not compared.

### 55 · The honest side of the Lean verifier's system is a constant one, the same at all weights

- **The limit.** Of the six top statements only `Sound`, in its nearest form, is proved of the Lean verifier's system, whose honest side is a constant one. `Sound` reads none of the constant honest side's fields, so the soundness theorem (28) is unaffected: what follows is about a system with the constant committer, no attack on the protocol and no doubt of it. `Sound` is one of the three properties that make up a doubly zero-knowledge commit-and-prove argument, and it is proved in a restricted form. Of the goals, those that read the honest side (G9, G9bits, G3'pol) are not proved of it (section 2.2). By (a) the goals' statements, taken together, do not hold of it [paper]. Another system with the same verifier, the compiled system at the honest part written in Lean, is told at 39.
  - The constant honest side supplies six of the system's ten fields: the committer commits to nothing (256 zero bits, at all weights), and the prover sends five empty files (Johnson/Goal/Tie/DescriptionOfData.lean:190-198; Johnson/Goal/Description/Honest.lean:21). The protocol's honest committer and its three honest provers are written in Lean, as definitions (39), but they are not this system's fields; no theorem ties them to a program. So correctness of extraction, binding of the commitment (in the opening form), no two openings, completeness and weight hiding cannot be stated with any content of this system (64, 10, 15).
  - (a) At this system binding of the commitment in the opening form (the second clause of `SoundAndBinding`) and no two openings (`NoTwoOpenings`, which is none of the six) are false at every error below 1 [paper]. So is correctness of extraction: one constant commitment and an empty list of queries leave the extractor nothing by which to tell two sets of weights apart [paper]. Completeness cannot hold, and zero knowledge (`ZeroKnowledge`) would hold trivially [paper]. The results with fixed-message provers of 15 are of another system, whose committer encodes the weights: 15 (8).
  - (b) `SoundAndBinding` (nobody opens the commitment to weights other than the extractor's), `SoundOpened` (an accepted output is the model's at the very weights the commitment is opened to) and the goals G9 and G9bits are proved of illustrative systems: the table below. Each of these keeps `verify`, which no built program runs (45), beside a committer written in Lean that no theorem ties to the programs. None of them has an honest prover. The first two are proved of the compiled system at the honest part too, at public files in use (`finalSoundAndBinding_value`, `finalSoundOpened_value`, AtFullSide/Face/FinalBindingAtTheValue.lean; (c); 39).
  - (c) Of the Lean verifier's own system and the illustrative systems of the table below, none has binding of the commitment (in the opening form) and index privacy (in either of its two forms, `HasIndexPrivacy` and `HasStrongIndexPrivacyWith`: 20) together. The lines `soundAndBinding` and `zeroKnowledgeOnCanServe` are of one system, the compiled system at the honest part. The second is `ZeroKnowledgeOn`: it is typed in the way of `HasStrongIndexPrivacyWith` (20), the second of these two forms, for a smaller relation, on a named set of requests, and with the 256-bit commitment as its key. `HasStrongIndexPrivacyWith` for that relation gives `ZeroKnowledgeOn` (`zeroKnowledgeOn_of_privacy`, VerifiableInference/ZeroKnowledgeOn.lean:73); no theorem leads back (39). Its corollary `zeroKnowledgeAtEveryRequest` is in another form than these two.
  - (d) Nothing is proved on whether `verify` accepts a commitment that an illustrative committer makes (Ladder.lean:169-170). Where the gate rejects the public files, `verify` rejects everything and the committer stands over empty data, not the public files' own (73).
  - (e) `theBound`: its row in the table.
  - (f) Nothing is proved about `cp_commit`: neither that the extractor returns the weights that it committed (correctness of extraction), nor that nobody opens one of its commitments to weights other than the extractor's (binding of the commitment, in the opening form) or to two sets of weights (no two openings), nor anything else that names it. No test of any of the three has been run; in particular, no test exists in which one commitment is opened two ways.
- **Layer.** The protocol as written in Lean: (a) to (e), on what Lean's statements mean at these systems. The tie between Lean and the programs: (f).
- **Why it matters.** What the soundness theorem states is index binding (`sound_iff`, 28): at given public files, but for its error, an accepted output is the model's at one set of weights, the extractor's, fixed before any proof; where the extractor gives none, every accept counts toward that error. It does not say, of a commitment that `cp_commit` makes, that these are the provider's weights (64). This gap matters to whoever reads the theorem as more than `Sound`.
- **Proved nearest to it.** The illustrative systems, in seven rows. All keep `verify`. None has a proved completeness or hiding, and only the last row's committer has been compared with a program.

  | System | Committer | Provers | In a line |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | `theWideDigits` | In Lean; honest sampler the constant 0; an opener may name any pads and salts | Empty | The statements on openings; G9, G9bits; no index privacy |
  | `coinsSystem`, `theWideOpen` | `coinsSystem`: the randomness is the coins themselves, and an opener may pick any. `theWideOpen`: the randomness is a number, and the sampler gives 0 | `coinsSystem`: free. `theWideOpen`: empty | Opening forms at files in scope; G9, G9bits; as the first row |
  | (e) `theBound` | One setting of coins: no randomness reaches the commitment | Free | G9, G9bits; its clause on openings says next to nothing |
  | `theWider`, with `member` | A family; the constant 0 is a member of it as sampler | A parameter | G9, G9bits; `completeOn_last` concludes at `member` (52); nothing on hiding |
  | `plainSystemAt` | `committerAt`, of the same make as the first row's | Free | Opening forms from one premise; extraction at fixed coins (64) |
  | `plainSystem` | The commitment at given coins, in the opener's hand | Free | Opening forms, and extraction at fixed coins, at files in scope that are read; the verifier is idle there |
  | `Bind.Cpp.theCpp` | `cppCommit`, on byte strings; models one part of `cp_commit`, tied to it by no theorem; positive theorems at `theCpp (theTwin g)` | A parameter | All three properties where the tests on the public files pass; the one compared with a program; not G9bits (72) |

  - `theWideDigits` (Johnson/Goal/Conditions/WideDigits.lean:165); verifier: `same_verifier_wide` (Johnson/Goal/TopOfThreeBinding.lean:105-106); sampler: `placeHolderNumberCoins` (Johnson/Goal/Conditions/WideOnto.lean:110), so whoever guesses the weights can compute the commitment and compare.

    Proved: `SoundAndBinding` and `SoundOpened` at exact match (`soundAndBinding_level_digits_of_verify_at`, `soundOpened_level_digits_of_verify_at`, Johnson/Goal/Skeleton/SoundOfVerifyMore.lean:90-104), each on no axiom beyond Lean's three standard ones, with the theorem's three hypotheses, at about 124 bits a hash query with a floor of about 2^-100 (`levelError`), and twice that error for the opened form. G9, G9bits (`G9_digits`, `G9bits_digits`, Johnson/Goal/Conditions/WideDigits.lean:181-183). Its extractor is the IOP's own (`same_extractor_wide`, Johnson/Goal/SameExtractorWide.lean:40); nothing of the proof depends on that.

    G8bits with G9bits at one extractor, with no premise left (`policy_and_binding_digits`, Johnson/Goal/PolicyAndBinding.lean:147; from `all_digits_of_two`, Johnson/Goal/Skeleton/TopOfTwoMore.lean:53, `keySound_holds` and `proofSound_of_verify`, Johnson/Goal/Skeleton/SoundOfVerifyGoals.lean:71): a verifier that rejects everything (52 (a)), or one that enforces exact match at every policy (6 (e)), meets that pair too. Correctness of extraction at each fixed choice of coins alone (`extracts_own_digits`, Johnson/Goal/Conditions/BindsOwn.lean:162). Index privacy, in either form, would not hold there even trivially: 15 (8) [paper].
  - `coinsSystem` (Johnson/Goal/Conditions/BindsAtCoins.lean:212) and `theWideOpen` (Johnson/Goal/Conditions/WideOnto.lean:119): binding of the commitment (in the opening form) and no two openings at files in scope (`bindsWith_coins_digits`, `noTwoOpenings_coins_digits`, Johnson/Goal/Conditions/BindsAtCoins.lean:306, :344); G9, G9bits (`G9_open`, `G9bits_open`, Johnson/Goal/Conditions/WideOnto.lean:139, :141). Each of the two lacks what the first row's system lacks.
  - `theBound` (Johnson/Goal/Conditions/BindsAtThe.lean:142): G9, G9bits at every value of its parameter (`TheCoins`: those parts of the honest side that binding does not fix), from one premise on the coding (`G9_theBound`, `G9bits_theBound`, Johnson/Goal/Conditions/BindsAtThe.lean:196, :205), which is a theorem (`hE_the`, Johnson/Goal/Description/AtBound.lean:43). An opener that picks its own coins is not in G9 and G9bits at `theBound`. No other entry rests on it.
  - `theWider` (Johnson/Goal/Conditions/WiderAtThe.lean:116), `member` (Johnson/Goal/Conditions/HomeAtWider.lean:192): G9, G9bits (`G9_theWider`, `G9bits_theWider`, Johnson/Goal/Conditions/WiderAtThe.lean:147, :154).
  - `plainSystemAt` (Johnson/Goal/Conditions/BindsAtSecondHashFunction.lean:212), `committerAt` (Johnson/Goal/Conditions/BindsAtSecondHashFunction.lean:201): binding of the commitment (in the opening form) and no two openings, from the one premise that the files are read (`bindsWith_atOlder`, `noTwoOpenings_atOlder`, Johnson/Goal/Conditions/BindsAtSecondHashFunction.lean:293, :331). `SoundAndBinding` at one set of public files in scope at exact match, from `KeySoundAt Z` and `ProofSoundAt Z` (`soundAndBinding_plain_at`, Johnson/Goal/Skeleton/SoundAndBindingPlain.lean:148), at the second hash function alone; it is not the goals' `NearestSoundAndBinding`. Correctness of extraction at each fixed choice of coins alone (`extracts_atOlder`, `extractor_atOlder`, Johnson/Goal/Conditions/BindsAtSecondHashFunction.lean:391, :257). In that theorem the leaves are strings outside the format [read] (64); binding in the opening form and no two openings are not affected by that.
  - `plainSystem` (Johnson/Goal/Conditions/BindsPlain.lean:327): `bindsWith_plain`, `extracts_plain` (at each fixed choice of coins), `noTwoOpenings_plain` (Johnson/Goal/Conditions/BindsPlain.lean:359, :415, :428). Its committer gives the same value as the last row's (72).
  - `Bind.Cpp.theCpp` (Johnson/Bind/Cpp/Defs.lean:390), `Bind.Cpp.cppCommit` (Johnson/Bind/Cpp/Defs.lean:242); verifier and extractor: `cpp_is_of_verify` (Johnson/Bind/Cpp/OfTie.lean:155).

    Correctness of extraction, binding of the commitment and no two openings, at files in scope where the tests on the public files pass (`extractsCommitted`, `bindsAt`, Johnson/Bind/Cpp/TheTie.lean:204, :216; `noTwoOpenings_live`, Johnson/Bind/Cpp/NoTwoOpeningsAtLive.lean:64); that they pass: 73. The pair (soundness for the files' policy with binding of the commitment, in the opening form) stands there as two theorems at one extractor term, with no join (`g8bits_cpp`, Johnson/Bind/Cpp/OfTie.lean:164; `bindsAt`), and as one theorem only at `zGap` (`policy_and_binding_at_zGap`, Johnson/Bind/Cpp/TheTie.lean:233), where these tests pass. Correctness of extraction is proved in the form of the library's own statement, `ExtractsCommittedWeights`, on average over sampled coins.

    Soundness after an honest commitment, for the files' policy at all files in scope that are read, at an error of its own, never below about 2^-88 (`soundAtCommitted`, Johnson/Bind/Cpp/SoundAtCommitted.lean:227). Where the tests on the public files fail it holds because `verify` then rejects everything, and the theorem does not say which case holds. That form is no test of a committer: the constant committer meets it for every claim, and no case in which it forbids anything is shown by theorems alone (72 (d), (e)). It is not the goal G9bits; further limits: 72.
  - Which forms name the extractor, the IOP's own: (77). To these, correctness of extraction at the same extractor can be joined at an illustrative system. At the rows of `theWideDigits` and `plainSystemAt` it is proved at each fixed choice of coins alone, where, but for a chance of at most 2^-256, the trees' leaves are strings outside the format [read] (64), and no theorem states the join. At `theCpp` the join is a theorem: soundness after an honest commitment, under the conditions of 72 (d), (e); at exact match the accepted output is the model's own at the weights put in (an accepted empty output: 72).
  - Of the Lean verifier's system the pair (G8bits with G9bits, at one extractor) is not written. Correctness of extraction is proved at `theCpp` at files in scope where the tests on the public files pass. That they pass is proved at the written copies in the side library, and in the main library not. Correctness of extraction is not proved of `cp_commit`.
- **Tested nearest to it.** One false copy's file of a cheating committer was rejected in a test of soundness (33) [evaluated]. That tests none of the three properties of (f).
  - `cppCommit` beside two runs of `cp_commit`: values compared, nothing proved. At the small stored example's public files, on synthetic weights, under one public seed, a faster text of that committer, proved equal to it, together with two further Lean texts, for the table and for the coins, of which no theorem on the committer speaks, gave all 2^23 leaf digests, the root, the 256 OOD answers and the commitment of two runs of `cp_commit` [evaluated]. That is an agreement of values, it tests none of the three properties of (f) (Johnson/Bind/README.md), and it cannot be repeated from this repository. Nothing compares the table at weights as the theorems take them, at any other size, under any other seed, or at a real model's weights.
- **Known attack or counterexample.** None known on the protocol; for hiding, with the conditions that the head of 15's appendix names. Where a Lean statement in (a) or at `theWideDigits` fails, or is vacuous or met trivially, as typed, that is a fault of the statement at that system, not of the protocol.

### 64 · "The committed weights" are what the extractor finds

- **The limit.** No theorem says that the extractor returns the committer's weights (correctness of extraction) at the Lean verifier's system, and none can: its committer commits to nothing (55). Nothing of the kind is proved of `cp_commit`. This does not affect the soundness bound of `verify`: it says which weights that bound is about.
  - (a) The extractor reads the commitment and the hash queries of whoever made the commitment ID. Where it finds no key in these queries, or the decoded table contains no weights in range, it gives nothing (Johnson/Goal/Description/Extractor.lean:35-39, :46-52; Johnson/Flat/Dec/PolicyAtFrame.lean:60-63; ZKCP/DigestKey.lean:94-100), and then every accept counts as a failure.
  - (b) It is a mathematical function, defined with choice. It is not shown to be computable or efficient.
  - (c) At this system, whose honest side is a constant one, no theorem says that `verify` accepts an honest proof under a sampled hash function, nor that the extractor then finds the committer's weights. Of the compiled system at the honest part the first is `completeAtWellFormedRequests` (39), and the second `finalExtracts_value` (below). At the real hash function, that it accepts is evaluated (52).
- **Layer.** The protocol as written in Lean: what the statement's extractor is. The tie between Lean and the programs: no theorem reaches `cp_commit`.
- **Why it matters.** `Sound` does not say that the prover knew the weights, and the notion's long name does not mean that either: the relation has no witness, and the extractor is an arbitrary function. No theorem excludes, for `cp_commit`, that an accept is about other weights than the provider put in, or that one commitment answers to two sets of weights. At `theCpp`, whose illustrative committer is a Lean committer that models one part of that program (no theorem ties them), both are bounded where the tests on the public files pass, with the limits of 72 (55, row `theCpp`).
- **Proved nearest to it.**
  - Which statements name an extractor, and which only say that there is one: (77).
  - For an illustrative committer (`committerAt`, at fixed coins; 55, row `plainSystemAt`), `extracts_atOlder` (Johnson/Goal/Conditions/BindsAtSecondHashFunction.lean:391): at any public files that are read, from the commitment and that run's hash queries the extractor gives that committer's weights back, but for an error of at most about 2^-155 (3·(3·2^48+2)²/2^256; arithmetic, no Lean theorem states the number). `extractor_atOlder` (Johnson/Goal/Conditions/BindsAtSecondHashFunction.lean:257) says that this extractor is the IOP's own.
  - That is correctness of extraction at each fixed setting of that committer's coins, with one bound for all settings. The format's own start value, the public files' digest, is itself an answer of the hash function, and coins fixed before the hash function is sampled equal it with probability at most 2^-256 [read]. So this theorem speaks of trees whose leaves are strings outside the format, not of a commitment that the programs could write.
  - The library's own statement, on average over sampled coins (`ExtractsCommittedWeights`, VerifiableInference/MoreStatements.lean:83-92), would follow by averaging, a step that no file takes for this committer (in a general form, for a committer that samples one number first: `OpensToExtracted.extractionError_coinsFirst`, ZKCP/WithOpeningsExtracts.lean:31). It is proved of one further illustrative system, where `protocolDataWellFormed` passes, at an error of about 2^-89 (`extractsCommitted`, Johnson/Bind/Cpp/TheTie.lean:204; 55, row `theCpp`), and of the compiled committer at the honest part, at public files in use, which stays within 2^48 queries, at about 2^-155 (`finalExtracts_value`, AtFullSide/Face/FinalBindingAtTheValue.lean).
  - None of this says whether `verify` accepts that commitment. Where the gate rejects the files, it speaks of a committer over empty data. That the gate accepts is a theorem at the written copy (73).
  - So `Sound`'s extractor is shown not to be hollow at the illustrative systems `plainSystemAt` and `theCpp` and at the compiled committer at the honest part, each under the conditions told above. It is not so shown at a commitment that the programs could write (78).

### 77 · Several forms at `verify` say only that an extractor exists

- **The limit.** `sound_of_verify_at`, `indexBinding_of_verify_at`, the executable form's `sound_of_the_form_events` (Johnson/PVR/SoundOfTheFormEvents.lean:252) and every form for a list of proofs at `verify` say only that there is an extractor, so a statement on what the extractor returns at an honest commitment cannot be joined to them.

  At illustrative systems a second statement is joined to soundness at one extractor. At exact match, binding of the commitment (in the opening form): at `theWideDigits` with no further hypothesis (`soundAndBinding_level_digits_of_verify_at`, Johnson/Goal/Skeleton/SoundOfVerifyMore.lean:90), and at `plainSystemAt` from two premises, `KeySoundAt Z` and `ProofSoundAt Z`, each a theorem elsewhere (`soundAndBinding_plain_at`, Johnson/Goal/Skeleton/SoundAndBindingPlain.lean:148). At these two no theorem joins correctness of extraction itself. At `theCpp` one does: soundness after an honest commitment, with the limits of 72 (d), (e) (55, those rows). Of the compiled system at the honest part, at public files in use, soundness and correctness of extraction are proved at one named extractor (`bindingOf_value`, `finalExtracts_value`, AtFullSide/Face/FinalBindingAtTheValue.lean; 10, 64; the system: 39).

  The forms under the files' policy name the extractor, the IOP's own (among them `soundUnderPolicy_of_verify_at`, Johnson/Goal/Skeleton/SoundOfVerifyPolicy.lean:63; `soundForGaps_of_verify_at`, Johnson/Goal/Skeleton/SoundOfVerifyGaps.lean:308; `indexBindingUnderPolicy_of_verify_at`, Johnson/Goal/Skeleton/SoundOfVerifyForms.lean:401), and so does `indexBindingWith_of_verify_at` at exact match (Johnson/Goal/Skeleton/SoundOfVerifyForms.lean:417); for a list of proofs no form at `verify` names it.
- **Layer.** The protocol as written in Lean: what these statements name.
- **Why it matters.** `Sound` asks for some extractor and itself names none. So correctness of extraction (64) cannot be joined to these statements. The bound of the soundness theorem (28) is unaffected: this is about which statements a further result can be joined to.
- **Proved nearest to it.**
  - Named: the forms of the third paragraph of "The limit". The statement of `soundUnderPolicy_of_verify_at` is at Johnson/Goal/Skeleton/TopAtZ.lean:70-78 (section 2.2); `indexBindingWith_of_verify_at` is at exact match.
  - `soundUnderPolicyAt_of_verify` and `g8bits_of_verify` (Johnson/Goal/Skeleton/SoundOfVerifyPolicy.lean:70, :75) follow from these and name it too.
  - Not named: `sound_of_verify_at` (Johnson/Goal/Skeleton/SoundOfVerifyAt.lean:69), `indexBinding_of_verify_at` (Johnson/Goal/Skeleton/SoundOfVerifyForms.lean:341), and the forms for a list (70). The general lemma for a list keeps a named extractor (`SoundForWith.manyProofsUnion`, Johnson/Goal/Skeleton/SoundOfVerifyForms.lean:204), but no form at `verify` is stated from it with the extractor named.

### 72 · The illustrative system `theCpp`: further limits

- **The limit.** This entry states the limits of the results on the commitment. `Bind.Cpp.theCpp` (Johnson/Bind/Cpp/Defs.lean:390) is the Lean verifier's system with an illustrative committer on byte strings (`Bind.Twin.commitW`, Johnson/Bind/Twin.lean:297; `Bind.Cpp.cppCommit`, Johnson/Bind/Cpp/Defs.lean:242) in the constant committer's place. It keeps `verify`. `theCpp` and `cppCommit` are families over a parameter: every positive theorem here is stated at `theCpp (theTwin g)`, and `blindTwin_fails` at `theCpp (blindTwin g)`. Of the Lean verifier's own system correctness of extraction, binding of the commitment (in the opening form) and no two openings are not stated. Of `theCpp` further limits hold:
  - (a) *The empty output.* At exact match the library's own form of soundness after an honest commitment, for the claim "the output is the model's", lets an accepted empty output pass: the empty output counts as the model's at all weights. The form for the files' policy bars it, and so does a form of its own for non-empty outputs (`soundAtCommitted_exact_nonempty`, Johnson/Bind/Cpp/SoundAtCommittedExact.lean:117).
