  - (b) *What the comparison of the two committers cannot see.* The properties reach this committer through a theorem that it and another committer written in Lean (the one of `plainSystem`, Johnson/Goal/Conditions/BindsPlain.lean) give the same value at every hash function, at the full size, where the plan's leaf salts have 40 bytes (a premise, in the main library). The encoder's symbol function, the frame of the hashed strings and the weights' digits are one Lean text on both sides, so an error in these shows on neither. Only the first two are reached from outside, by the evaluations at a small shape (55). The weights' digits are compared with the programs' nowhere.
  - (c) *No goal is met.* The theorem on binding of the commitment (in the opening form) is not the goal G9bits: that goal asks for all files in scope that are read, and the theorem reaches only those at which the tests on the public files pass. Soundness after an honest commitment is none of the goals.
  - (d) *Soundness after an honest commitment is no test of a committer.* That form bounds accepts alone, so a committer under whose commitments `verify` accepts nothing meets it. The constant committer of the Lean verifier's own system meets it for every claim, at all files in scope that are read (`Bind.Cpp.hollow_meets_join`, Johnson/Bind/Cpp/JoinControls.lean:109).

    What separates `cppCommit` from the constant committer, where the plan's leaf salts have 40 bytes (`SaltAt`, Johnson/Bind/Cpp/Defs.lean:298), is correctness of extraction: a committer in its place that does not read the weights fails that at every error below 1/2, whatever the extractor (`Bind.Cpp.blind_extracts`, Johnson/Bind/Cpp/Controls.lean:320). If it makes at most 2^35 queries, it fails binding of the commitment (in the opening form) at `theCpp`'s own extractor and at the one error `bindingErrorInQueries` (`blindDoesNotBind`, Johnson/Bind/Cpp/Controls.lean:275, stated at Johnson/Bind/Cpp/Guards.lean:173-182), and no two openings at every error below 1 at 2^35 + 2 queries (`blind_twoOpenings`, Johnson/Bind/Cpp/Controls.lean:309). All of these ask that the files are read, none that they are in scope. One such committer is written out (`blindTwin_fails`, Johnson/Bind/Cpp/Controls.lean:346). A committer that does not read the weights and makes more queries is not reached by the two on openings (Johnson/Bind/Cpp/Controls.lean:52-54). That the constant committer fails correctness of extraction is [paper] (55 (a)).

    No theorem shows that a committer that does not read the weights fails soundness after an honest commitment itself. That this form, taken beside completeness, would separate `cppCommit` from such a committer is proved only for committers that do not read the weights and keep the count of queries, with completeness as a premise, which is proved of no such system (`blind_join_and_complete`, Johnson/Bind/Cpp/JoinControls.lean:152), and, with no further premise on the request, only for the claim "the output is the model's" (`blind_exact_join_and_complete`, Johnson/Bind/Cpp/JoinControls.lean:183). A committer that reads the weights and places them wrongly, or reads some of them, is not covered. Beyond these it is [paper].
  - (e) *The error of soundness after an honest commitment, and where it forbids anything.* `soundAtCommitted_own` (Johnson/Bind/Cpp/SoundAtCommitted.lean:323): an output that `verify` accepts meets the files' policy at the committed weights, which at some policies in scope bars nothing (6, 44), except on an event of probability about 2^-88 + p/2^124, on average over the committer's seed, after p queries of the prover, who is given that seed; the committer's own 2^35 queries are charged. That is 124 bits for each query of the prover, and never below about 2^-88.

    Correctness of extraction holds up to about 2^-89 at the committer's own 2^35 queries, about 2^-156 at the square form (`extractsCommitted_square_salt`, Johnson/Bind/Cpp/SoundAtCommitted.lean:247), and at every randomness too (`extracts_salt`, Johnson/Bind/Cpp/TheTie.lean:181); binding of the commitment (in the opening form) at about (t + 2^23)/2^124 after t queries, never below about 2^-101; no two openings at twice that. For no two openings the committer's two runs are not among the adversary's queries, and nothing is said of two openings with the same weights and different randomness (VerifiableInference/MoreStatements.lean:208-209; Johnson/Bind/Cpp/NoTwoOpeningsAtLive.lean:29-30). No figure is 128 bits.

    Soundness after an honest commitment reaches all files in scope that are read only because, where a test on the public files fails, `verify` rejects everything. The committer returns the commitment alone, with no key file, and no honest prover is written for `theCpp`: no theorem shows `verify` accepting a proof under such a commitment, so no case in which the statement forbids anything is shown by theorems alone. That a given commitment was made by this committer is outside the statement. It speaks of one commitment and one proof, as a joint probability, not a conditional one (Johnson/Bind/Cpp/SoundAtCommitted.lean:28); the weights, the public files and the prover's strategy are fixed before the hash function is sampled, and the second hash function is one fixed function there (Johnson/Bind/Cpp/SoundAtCommitted.lean:108).
  - (f) *Where the statements stand.* They stand at the full size alone, where this committer's text cannot be run (about 2^72 multiplications: Johnson/Bind/Cpp/Defs.lean:107-108). `verify` stands at the full size alone too, so no run at any size tries these statements: the evaluations at a small shape (55) compare values of the committer's definition and try no theorem. How a seed becomes coins is a free parameter of the theorems (`g`). How weights become the table is the library's own, compared with the programs' nowhere.
- **Layer.** The protocol as written in Lean. (b) and the end of (f) also touch the tie between Lean and the programs.
- **Why it matters.** It affects no statement on the soundness of `verify`. It limits what `theCpp` can be taken to show of `cp_commit`, to which no theorem ties it.
- **Proved nearest to it.** 55, row `theCpp`; (64). Of what these results do not reach: (78).
- **Known attack or counterexample.** None known.

### 78 · Results on illustrative committers speak only of their own commitments

- **The limit.** The results on illustrative committers speak only of commitments that those Lean committers make. Under a commitment of any other make the extractor may return any weights. It may also return none. No theorem says that a commitment under which `verify` accepts is of an illustrative committer's make (Johnson/Bind/Cpp/TheTie.lean:229-230), and the goals allow a verifier that accepts commitments of another make (Ladder.lean:170-172). A commitment of a committer's make is meant by value: one whose value that committer gives at some weights and randomness.
- **Layer.** The protocol as written in Lean: of which commitments these theorems speak. The tie between Lean and the programs: no theorem reaches a commitment that the programs write.
- **Why it matters.** No theorem ties these committers to the programs (55, 72). So these results do not reach a commitment that the programs write (64). The soundness bound of `verify` (28) holds at whatever weights the extractor returns; what is not proved is that these are the provider's (64).
- **Proved nearest to it.** `extracts_atOlder` (Johnson/Goal/Conditions/BindsAtSecondHashFunction.lean:391) and `extractsCommitted` (Johnson/Bind/Cpp/TheTie.lean:204), each of its own committer; the rest: the table in 55.

### 36 · What the level means: three quantities

- **The limit.** At files in scope where `verify`'s tests pass, each round of the Lean verifier's protocol is proved to err at most 2^-128 over all its challenges, proof-of-work bits included, within the limits told under "Proved nearest to it"; a whole proof's error is (c). Neither the soundness theorem nor any of the six lines states 2^-128 for a round, and none of this is said of the C++ programs. Three quantities go by the name of the level, and they differ.
  - (a) *A round*: at most about 2^-128, with proof of work counted. The step to the whole error (28 (c)) asks that the named bound on a round's error be at most 2/2^128 (`RoundErrorSmall`, Johnson/Goal/Conditions/RowsAtTheRecord.lean:20-22); that named bound is 1/2^128 (`eps_the`, below).
  - (b) *A hash query*: about 2^-124. That is what the goals with a level state: 124 bits for each query, not 128 (Ladder/Bits.lean:10-18).
  - (c) *A whole attack*: about (t + n + 2^23) / 2^124, for one commitment, one prompt, one output, one proof and one call of `verify` (Ladder/Bits.lean:61-64, 94-98). The 2^23 is a fixed allowance for the verifier's own queries, so the bound is never below about 2^-101, even at no query. For a list of N proofs what is proved is N times this bound; the form in which each further proof is paid for in queries has a premise that no theorem gives (70).
  - (d) *The units.* t is the committer's and the prover's queries added up, n the proof parser's (n = 1 in the theorem). Both count queries to the model's hash function, which returns 32 bytes a query [read]. A real BLAKE2b call that returns 32 bytes (Merkle leaves and nodes, digests of files) is one query; a call that returns 64 bytes (each Fiat-Shamir challenge call, a proof-of-work attempt among them) is two [read]. So Q real calls are at most 2Q queries, and 124 bits per query is at least 123 bits per real call [read].
  - (e) *The allowance.* The 2^23 is counted in neither of these units [read]. It is counted mostly in typed queries, so the sum t + n + `verifierQueryAllowance` adds quantities in different units, and no goal bounds the number of strings that the verifier hashes (Ladder/Bits.lean:38-45). One class of typed query is at least 141 strings. The verifier's own queries as the compiler counts them, in typed queries (one for each round, each opened leaf and each tree node), are about 716,000 at the full size [counted]. That is no count of calls of the hash function: a round's query is answered by many hash outputs, so the verifier calls the hash function more often than that. The bound does not need the number of calls small.
  - (f) *Proof of work.* At the 71 rounds that sample positions (50 distinct ones across the seven chains) 20, 28 or 32 of the 128 bits are proof of work [counted]. These rounds reach 2^-128 only with it; the narrowest stands at 2^-128.10 [counted]. Without it the key file's and the copy's file's stand at about 2^-96, and the proof file's between 2^-100 and 2^-110 [counted]. Multiplying by 2^-work is the random-oracle model's count. Against the real hash function it is the usual assumption on the cost of grinding. The goals count a proof-of-work attempt as a query, and ask a round whose error is small only with its work to hold that work (Ladder/Bits.lean:15-17). How the laws discharge that, round by round, is not known; the kernel has checked the theorem either way.
  - (g) *The figures of docs/limits.md.* The typed error is looser than every figure with proof of work counted in docs/limits.md, so the theorem backs none of these figures [read].
  - (h) *The goals of Ladder.lean.* They ask only for an error of 1/4 (`ceiling`, Ladder.lean:255-256), which a verifier that ignores the request at one hash function in eight would meet.
- **Layer.** The protocol as written in Lean.
- **Why it matters.** Bits of single rounds are not the chance of a forgery. Somebody who relies on an accept has the theorem's whole error, (c), and not 128 bits.
- **Proved nearest to it.**
  - The error of `sound_of_verify_at` (Johnson/Goal/Skeleton/SoundOfVerifyAt.lean:69) grows with t. It is about 2^-101 at t = 0, 2^-84 at t = 2^40, 2^-60 at t = 2^64, 2^-44 at t = 2^80 and 2^-4 at t = 2^120. From about 2^124 queries on the bound says nothing. A value of the error is to be quoted together with the number of queries at which it is taken. The theorem is stated at that error function, so it yields (b) and (c).
  - Beneath it, at every set of public files in scope at which `verify`'s own tests on those files pass, three theorems bound the error of every round of the key file, the copy's file and the proof file by 1/2^128, for the relation that each file's own statement gives (Johnson/Top/InnerCompiledVerifier.lean:154-173): for the key file, that the word has a collision at the OOD seed or decodes with the OOD sample; for the copy's file, that the first word does not decode, or the further word has a collision, or both decode to the same index; for the proof file, the relaxed relation at the decoded index.
  - The bound is the named `VerifierAndGoals.AtTheName.eps_the` (Johnson/Goal/Conditions/RowsAtTheRecord.lean:176-180), which is 1/2^128 by its definition and says nothing of the protocol by itself. It is met by `VerifierAndGoals.AtTheName.keySound_at_live_holds` (Johnson/Goal/Conditions/KeyOpenHolds.lean:95-98), `FlashProve.entrySound` (Johnson/Goal/Conditions/EntrySits.lean:52) and `VerifierAndGoals.AtTheName.proofSoundAt_of_verify` (Johnson/Goal/Skeleton/SoundOfVerifyMore.lean:69-71), each proved. Each is met too by a decision that never accepts, so any of the three may be true and vacuous (52 (a)). All three rest on the library's own proof of the statement on codes (12).
  - That those tests pass is proved at the written copy, and at that copy with only the policy changed (73). How many of the 128 bits are proof of work at the sampling rounds, 20 to 32, is arithmetic: (f).
  - That the compiler's count of typed queries is at most 2^20 at all public files is proved (`VerifierAndGoals.AtTheName.count_the`, Johnson/Goal/Conditions/RowsAtTheRecord.lean:136-137).

### 5 · The public files are fixed before the hash function is sampled

- **The limit.** The public files are fixed before the hash function is sampled, but the provider, who is the prover, writes the scale file and eight numbers of the parameter file.
  - A provider that picks or grinds its files with the hash function in hand, as every provider can, is covered by no goal (Ladder.lean:149-151).
  - What guards against it is that the files' digest comes first in every chain of queries [read]. No goal can tell a verifier that puts the digest first from one that does not.
  - The protocol as Lean describes it is written with a fixed value (32 bytes of 0x01) where the digest would be (section 2.4). The true digest enters through the coding, the OOD seed's query and the commitment's code [read], and no theorem says that every challenge depends on it.
- **Layer.** The protocol as written in Lean.
- **Why it matters.** A provider who tunes the public files with the hash function in hand is outside every statement. A user has to hold and read the public files (54, 69), and even so has no theorem against files that were chosen after hash queries.
- **Tested nearest to it.** In one run (of which kind is not recorded) with a public file altered and the commitment ID recomputed, all three parts rejected.
- **Known attack or counterexample.** None known.

### 69 · What fixes the weights: the commitment ID, the committer's hash queries and the public files, together

- **The limit.** No theorem bars one commitment ID from being accepted under two sets of files, with other weights extracted at each. Index binding, with its error, is had at given files alone.
  - In the definition that this library follows (ZKCP/Defs.lean) the index verification key is all that the committer publishes. In the theorem it is the 256-bit commitment ID alone (10), and the public files are not part of it (TERMS.md).
  - The three public files, of which whoever commits (the provider of (5)) writes the scale file whole and eight numbers of the parameter file, are outside the key.
  - Every theorem speaks of one set of files, fixed beforehand. The extractor takes the files as an argument, beside the ID and the committer's logged queries (Ladder.lean:365-369).
  - Every goal speaks of one set of files at a time, and `commit` may ignore them: one commitment may then be accepted under all public files in scope (Ladder.lean:161-163, point 13 of the comment there; the rest of point 13: 54 (f), and 54, "Why it matters").
  - The theorem's name, index-binding knowledge soundness, says neither that anybody knew the weights nor that one commitment stands for the same weights at other public files.
- **Layer.** The protocol as written in Lean.
- **Why it matters.** No theorem ties a commitment ID to one set of files. A user has to hold the right public files as it holds the right commitment (Ladder.lean:161-163). Of the differences from the definition (28) this gap is the one that bears on what the key fixes.
- **Tested nearest to it.** All of this field but its last clause is [read]. What guards against one ID with two sets of files: a digest of the files' bytes is hashed into the ID (54; docs/format/FORMAT.md, R4.11). That digest enters the commitment that `verify` checks (`configString`, `readConfig`, Ladder.lean:322-326), so that an honest commitment made for other files would not verify [paper].
- **Known attack or counterexample.** None known.

### 70 · One proof at a time; a list of proofs at N times the error

- **The limit.** The soundness theorem (28) bounds one accepted proof. For a list of at most N proofs, at public files in scope that are read and state exact match, what is proved is the union bound's error, `N * soundnessErrorInQueries (tC + tP) 1`, never below about N * 2^-101. The factor N is the union bound's; the two theorems that do without it say nothing as they stand ((c)).
  - (a) The experiment has one committer and one prover, which hands in one list of requests with proofs, at once. tP bounds the prover's hash queries for the whole list; tC is the committer's. The prover sees no decision of the verifier before it hands the list in, and it sees no honest proof. The extractor is fixed before N. In `SoundManyProofs`' form entries after the first N are not checked; in the session-augmented form a list of more than N entries is never counted as a failure (Johnson/Goal/Skeleton/SoundOfVerifyForms.lean:38-49).
  - (b) N separate sessions, each with its own bound tP, are another experiment. Put into this one, if the prover sees no decision between sessions, that prover has N * tP queries and the bound reads `N * soundnessErrorInQueries (tC + N * tP) 1` [paper]. A prover that sees decisions between sessions is not covered by that argument.
  - (c) Two further theorems state the forms for a list with no factor N in front, at the error `soundnessErrorInQueries (t + N * q) 1`, where t = tC + tP, at the same files (`soundManyProofs_of_verify_at`, `sessionAugmented_of_verify_at`, Johnson/Goal/Skeleton/SoundOfVerifyForms.lean:370-378, 381-395). Each has one premise more: q bounds the hash queries of one run of `verify`, at every commitment, request and proof. No theorem of this library gives such a q (what is proved counts typed queries, not hash queries: 36 (e)), so these two, as they stand, say nothing.
  - (d) For a list, no form at `verify` names the extractor, so a statement on what it returns at an honest commitment cannot be joined to these forms (77). Where a form names it, what can be joined at an illustrative system: 55's table. Under other policies: (6).
  - (e) The theorems at `verify` of Johnson/Goal/Skeleton/SoundOfVerifyForms.lean (the four of this entry: the two of (c) and the two corollaries under "Proved nearest to it"; and the index-binding forms of 28 and 77) are worth what `sound_of_verify_at` (Johnson/Goal/Skeleton/SoundOfVerifyAt.lean:69) and `soundUnderPolicy_of_verify_at` (Johnson/Goal/Skeleton/SoundOfVerifyPolicy.lean:63) are worth, and they prove nothing beneath `verify`. So they are about `verify`, not the C++ verifier (section 1); soundness only (55); at the full size alone, at the verifier with its fourteen entry tests and at the IOP's own parser (28); vacuous where the gate rejects (52 (a)); at files fixed beforehand (5, 69), at the second hash function (60), and with no bar on an accepted empty output (54). That an honest proof is accepted: 39, 52.
  - (f) What they state are this library's Lean formalizations. That a formalization is that of the definition that this library follows (ZKCP/Defs.lean) rests on a comparison [read]. `SoundManyProofs` is the library's own form, and session-augmented index binding is an optional addition to the definition.
- **Layer.** The protocol as written in Lean.
- **Why it matters.** Somebody who relies on many accepts under one commitment ID has the union bound at most, for a list handed in at once. Sessions in which the prover sees decisions are covered by no theorem and by no argument here.
- **Proved nearest to it.** At public files in scope that are read and state exact match, for a list of at most N proofs that one prover hands in at once under one commitment ID, two corollaries state a union bound at `verify`: `soundManyProofs_union_of_verify_at` and `sessionAugmented_union_of_verify_at` (Johnson/Goal/Skeleton/SoundOfVerifyForms.lean:352-357, 360-367). That file states no form for a list at `verify` under another policy: its general lemma for every claim (`SoundForWith.manyProofsUnion`, Johnson/Goal/Skeleton/SoundOfVerifyForms.lean:204-209) is applied to `verify` there at the model's own output alone (Johnson/Goal/Skeleton/SoundOfVerifyForms.lean:272-275, 357).
- **Known attack or counterexample.** None known.

### 71 · "No two answers" is not "no two openings"

- **The limit.** The corollary `noTwoAnswers_of_verify_at` (Johnson/Goal/Skeleton/NoTwoAnswersOfVerify.lean:97) does not say that an accepted output is the output of the weights that an honest committer put in (correctness of extraction). It does not say that a commitment can be opened only to the extractor's weights (binding of the commitment, in the opening form). It does not say that nobody opens one commitment to two sets of weights (no two openings).
  - It says nothing under a tolerant policy, nothing on two proofs under two commitments or at two sets of public files, and nothing on outputs of different lengths. That the shorter of two accepted outputs on one prompt is a prefix of the longer, at the same error, is an argument [paper] (Johnson/Goal/Skeleton/NoTwoAnswersOfVerify.lean:32-35).
  - The two accepted outputs of which the corollary speaks ("Proved nearest to it") belong to entries of one list of proofs; N bounds the length of the whole list, not the number of proofs that collide. The prover hands in that list at once, sees no decision of the verifier before, and sees no honest proof.
  - No honest committer's algorithm is read, and two sets of weights with the same outputs are not told apart.
  - `ZKCP.NoTwoAnswers` is the library's own notion and no part of the definition that this library follows (ZKCP/Defs.lean).
  - The limits of 70 (e) hold of it too.
- **Layer.** The protocol as written in Lean.
- **Why it matters.** Somebody who takes this corollary for correctness of extraction, binding of the commitment (in the opening form) or no two openings relies on more than it says.
- **Proved nearest to it.** `VerifierAndGoals.AtTheName.noTwoAnswers_of_verify_at` is a consequence of index-binding knowledge soundness (the terms, "Soundness"). Under one commitment and one set of public files that state exact match, no prover gets two different outputs of the same length accepted for one prompt, except with probability at most N times the soundness theorem's error, for a list of N proofs handed in at once, the error taken at the query budget of the whole list.
- **Known attack or counterexample.** None known.

### 60 · The second hash function occurs in the statement as a concrete function

- **The limit.** All goals but two read the IOP at a second, fixed hash function, built on BLAKE2b's compression function (`atSecondHashFunction`, Ladder.lean:461-463), and so does the theorem: 24 definitions of BLAKE2b's compression function occur in what the statement unfolds to. So the premise that the hash function is a random oracle holds of the main hash function alone.
  - The goals that ask the same whatever function is in the second hash function's place (G7', G7'bits) are not reached: security at any other function in that place is not proved.
  - That the second hash function does not interact with the sampled one is assumed outside Lean. LIMITS.md assumes with it that the second hash function resists collisions: which four look-up tables a parameter file names rests on that alone. It is a condition on the public files, not a hypothesis of the theorem ((i), below).
- **Layer.** The protocol as written in Lean.
- **Why it matters.** The question is whether soundness then rests on collision resistance of a non-standard hash function.
  - (i) By the statement, no: no hypothesis speaks of the second hash function, and the theorem is of that fixed function as it stands.
  - (ii) Beside the public tables, the second hash function digests the request, which the prover picks, and that digest is compared with nothing [read in part: the comparisons found; the digest's further way not followed]. The digest occurs in the hashed string of the first query, the verifier computes it itself, and no step of the proof reads it (SOUNDNESS-PROOF-MAP.md) [read].
  - (iii) What would go wrong otherwise is stated in a comment of the goals' file, which is no theorem: an IOP that ties the request through the second hash function alone fails the goals wherever that function has a collision between a true request and a false one (Ladder.lean:541-545). How the request does enter the hash: SOUNDNESS-PROOF-MAP.md.

### 7 · Lean's text of BLAKE2b

- **The limit.** That Lean's text of BLAKE2b is BLAKE2b rests on known answers alone (LIMITS.md). That BLAKE2b may be treated as a random oracle is assumed. The second hash function lies outside BLAKE2b's published analysis.
- **Layer.** The protocol as written in Lean: what it assumes of its hash functions.
- **Tested nearest to it.** The tables of Lean's text, lifted by program out of Lean, give a standard library's BLAKE2b-512 at 12 messages, and every single altered entry is rejected [tested].

### 51 · That `verify` behaves alike at a sampled and at the real hash function is read and searched, not proved

- **The limit.** The statement speaks of a sampled hash function, and no theorem says that `verify` behaves at the real hash function as at a sampled one. There are two lines of attack.
  - (a) *Vacuous.* The gate, or a decision, could depend on the hash function so that `verify` rejects at all but a vanishing share of hash functions, and `Sound` would hold for that reason.
  - (b) *A back door.* An IOP `D` whose verifier accepts more where the sampled hash function has, at one input, the real one's value loses nothing, because that value has probability 2^-256. At the real hash function the value can be chosen to occur. Such a `D` meets every goal in Ladder.lean and Ladder/Bits.lean (Ladder.lean:137-144). Whether a given `D` is such a one is a matter of reading `D`.
- **Layer.** The protocol as written in Lean.
- **Why it matters.** The theorem bounds an accept at a sampled hash function, and a user runs the real one. If `verify` compared a value of the sampled hash function with a stored value of the real one, it would reject under almost every sample, and the theorem would hold vacuously while every run at the real hash function passes. Or the same comparison could open a back door: a verifier that accepts anything where the sampled function agrees with the real one at one input also meets the theorem, and at the real hash function it would accept false claims ((b), above). It matters the more because the form of the theorem on `innerCompiledVerifier` with digests as answers, through which the proof goes, says something of a sampled hash function only where the IOP names no answer of it. That this condition is met is checked by name alone [read] (28 (a), in section 2.4).
- **Proved nearest to it.** On (a): at public files in use, at every well-formed request whose claimed output is the model's own result, `verify` rejects the Lean prover's proof with probability at most 2^-40 over the hash function and the coins (`completeAtWellFormedRequests`: 52). By its type the gate cannot depend on the sampled hash function. `protocolDataWellFormedAt (o : VerifierAndGoals.SecondHashFunctionType) (Zk : OnBytes.K.Pub) : Bool` takes the second hash function and the public files alone (Johnson/Goal/Description/Live.lean:69).
- **Tested nearest to it.** No comparison of an answer of the sampled hash function with a digest stored in the protocol's definition or with bytes of the public files was found (tests against 0 and proof of work: the next bullet), and nothing reads the one typed digest found beneath `verify`, `recordCfg` [read]. The compiler's verifier does compare such answers with the commitment and with digests in the proof, as a verifier of this kind must (for example `verifyDigestKey`, ZKCP/DigestKey.lean:66-71).
  - On (a), the three decisions after the gate: no fault found [read in part: seven kinds of sub-protocol not read for this, four by test names and searches alone]. At honest files five kinds of rejection do depend on the sampled hash function: the approximate range proof's range; an all-zero column of the sampled matrix; a batching coefficient that is 0; a sampled value that is 0; proof of work [read]. They are the format's own. Each is rare by a count by hand, one by a comment alone. For the Lean prover's proofs alone, Lean bounds them together with every other cause of a rejection at 2^-40 (`completeAtWellFormedRequests`, with its conditions: 52). No run has shown an accept of `verify` at any hash function but the real one.
  - On (b), no such comparison in `verify` was found [read] [counted]. The main hash function is not named beneath `verify`; the second hash function occurs there in its named place, and the concrete hashing code is reached only through it [counted]. Of 39 comparisons of bytes or digests that were read, none sets an answer of the sampled hash function against a digest stored in the protocol's definition or against bytes of the public files [read]. Of the five comparisons that go through the second hash function none has an answer of the sampled hash function, or anything computed from one, on either side [read].
  - One written value of the main hash function does occur there: 32 typed bytes, a digest of the stored full-size public files (`recordCfg`, OnBytes/Params/EntryDryRecord.lean:53-55). That it equals the format's concrete main hash function's answer, at these files, to a query that in the model goes to the random oracle is a `#guard` (Ladder/Guards.lean:62-66) [evaluated]. By point 10 of the opening comment of Ladder.lean (Ladder.lean:137-144) such a back door could be made of such a value.
  - Nothing reads that value [read]: the typed digest is overwritten by the sampled one at five places (`linkAtCfg`, OnBytes/Tie/EntryAtCfg.lean:115-117), projected away at two places, and at one left in a field that the proof drops unread, before anything is compared or hashed (28 (a)). A walk finds where it occurs (87 constants beneath `verify` depend on it, and none of the 39 comparisons has it on a side) but cannot show that nothing reads it [counted]. No theorem says that the outcome is the same with other bytes in the place of these 32 (Johnson/Top/Pub/ArmPublicTopFull.lean:254-259). It is the only such value that was found.
  - Over the terms beneath `verify` (21,516 constants) one constant alone contains a hex literal of 32 or more digits. The number literals of 2^64 or more are the model's five tables and a list of error bounds. Of the 172 constants that contain eight or more small numbers, one is a written answer of a hash function, and it is the second hash function's (the four public tables' digests: `refTableWords`, Johnson/Flat/SettingsRead.lean:124-130, with which the parameter file's lines on the look-up tables are compared when the files are parsed; that function is fixed in the statement, not sampled). Of the other 171 the names were read, not the values [counted].
  - Of the definitions of this library that contain a quoted value of 64 hex digits (43 in this repository [counted]; of the same make as `recordCfg` is `smallRecordCfg`, a typed digest of another set of public files), `recordCfg` alone stood on the list of the 204,124 constants on which the theorem depended in a build of Lean files that differ from this repository's, which had 44 of them [counted].
  - No walk can test equality with a hash function's answer, or see a value that code makes from small parts. A walk over names cannot see what is computed rather than named, nor a literal answer.
- **Known attack or counterexample.** None known: no fault found. (b) is closed by no theorem; what is proved on (a): "Proved nearest to it".

### 35 · The scope of every statement

- **The limit.** Every statement is scoped to the stored full-size layout file and parameter file, byte for byte but for eight numbers, with any lawful scale file whose rotary flags follow the reference pattern. The goal on completeness under a policy (G3'pol) is asked at two named sets alone; the line `completeOnCanServe` speaks at public files in use (39). A model whose fit changes one of the fixed lines cannot be verified by this verifier at all (Ladder.lean:77-93, 297-317).
  - The programs' reader accepts public files that the scope excludes; `verify` rejects them, and no statement covers them. One case is named: another pattern of rotary flags (Inst/Model/ProgTests.lean:238-239, 262-265; Lean's port of the programs' reader asks only 0 or 1: OnBytes/Model/LayerLayout.lean:808).
  - That the programs' reader admits that case rests on Lean's port of it and on one reading of the programs [read]. Beyond that one reading none of this has been compared with the C++ source.
  - The other way round, Lean's port of the programs' reader admits values of the parameter file that the programs refuse. `cp_verify` and the provers refuse a parameter file whose line `reading`, `rowcost` or `answers` is not 1, or whose line `listed` is not 0, on every kind of weights, and a file at prof 19; the port's reader admits `reading` and `rowcost` 1 or 2, `answers` 1 to 4 and `listed` 0 to 8 (OnBytes/Params/File.lean:284-297), and of prof it asks only that it lies from 16 to 31 (OnBytes/Model/Config.lean:664-666).

    At `reading 2`, at `answers` 2 to 4 and at another profile `verify-on-bytes` ends without a pass; none of its twenty-one conditions of the standard parameter form reads `rowcost` or `listed` (`servedOf`, OnBytes/Model/VerifyStages.lean:239): under such a file, if its rounds meet their target (the parameter file's line `sec`: OnBytes/Params/File.lean:72-73) at the dearer count (`rowcost 2` counts a factor of the number of rows: OnBytes/Params/File.lean:78-79), `verify-on-bytes` could pass where `cp_verify` refuses [read: in both sources; not tried] (OnBytes/LIMITS.md, point 16). With the released provers no proof can be made under such a parameter file: they refuse it too [read]. That is of `verify-on-bytes`, not of `verify`, which refuses such files: `reading 1`, `rowcost 1`, `answers 1`, `listed 0` and `prof 20` are fixed lines of the stored parameter file (Ladder/Stored.lean:35-46, :68), and none of them is among the eight free numbers (`FreeLines`, Ladder/Stored.lean:226-234).
- **Layer.** The protocol as written in Lean: the scope of the statements. The tie between Lean and the programs: the files that the programs' reader accepts and `verify` rejects.
- **Why it matters.** The scope bounds what any theorem here can mean. The provider writes the public files, so these files are the proving side's choice (5). A user who checks the public files against the reference is within the theorems' scope; one who accepts other files is outside it.
- **Known attack or counterexample.** None known.

### 50 · The files that state the goals

- **The limit.** What lies beneath the goals in the library is not pinned (18), and neither is one further file, Ladder/FitBinds.lean, that lies on the goals' side of the line. A goal is a statement that this library asks of an IOP (Terms used on this page).
  - `scripts/check.py` pins twenty-nine files by checksum, all alike, byte for byte. Some of them state the goals, or statements, definitions and stored terms that the goals are written in. Some state forms in which none of these goals is written, among them VerifiableInference/StatementsNonEmpty.lean, further forms of the six top statements (39, "The six top statements as written", (c)); VerifiableInference/AtEveryRequest.lean and VerifiableInference/WellFormedRequests.lean (below); and VerifiableInference/Steps.lean, the form `CompleteOn`, in which the line `completeOnCanServe` is written (39); theorems in other pinned files tie that form to `ProofAcceptedWhere`, in which no goal is written, but the field `complete` of `FinalStatementOneBit` is (among them `completeOn_iff_proofAccepted`, Ladder/Ties.lean, and `finalComplete_iff_completeOn`, Johnson/Face/Checks.lean) [read].

    The others state no goal: they contain theorems and tests about the goals and about what the goals are written in, and a few definitions of their own, some named by theorems (the error term `levelError`, Ladder/PackageBits.lean:99, is one) and some only by tests; among them is `ArgumentSystem.ofArgument` (VerifiableInference/OfArgument.lean:62), on which the goals' verifier is built [read].

    The field `complete` of `FinalStatementOneBit` (`FinalComplete`, through `CompleteAtFiles`, Johnson/Face/FinalStatement.lean) is written in `ProofAcceptedWhere` (VerifiableInference/AtEveryRequest.lean), `WellFormedRequest` and `modelResult` (VerifiableInference/WellFormedRequests.lean). These files, VerifiableInference/AtEveryRequestGuards.lean and VerifiableInference/RequestResult.lean beside them, the three under Johnson/Face/ that define the fields of `FinalStatementOneBit`, and the file that states the six lines (AtFullSide/Face/TheSixLines.lean) are among the twenty-nine; the checksums of these pins are taken from this repository's own bytes, so over this repository they cannot fail [counted: the eight rows against this repository's files]. The statements of the six lines are among those that scripts/front-statements.txt stores (section 6, "Checks"; on that file: 81 (a)).

    The checksums of fifteen of the other pins (Ladder.lean, Ladder/Bits.lean, Ladder/Guards.lean, Ladder/Package.lean, Ladder/Stored.lean, Ladder/Ties.lean, Ladder/BitsTies.lean, Ladder/NonEmptyTies.lean, Ladder/PackageBits.lean, Ladder/PackageTies.lean, VerifiableInference/Bridge.lean, VerifiableInference/CanServe.lean, VerifiableInference/ModelWeights.lean, VerifiableInference/StatementsNonEmpty.lean, VerifiableInference/Final/ZeroKnowledgeAtTheProtocol.lean) are taken from this repository's own bytes as well; for these the same holds [counted: against a script that is not this repository's].

    Which file is of which kind: LIMITS.md, L76. The list, with a SHA-256 checksum for each, is `GOAL_SUMS_TEXT` (scripts/check.py). `goals_stand` (scripts/check.py) compares every byte of them with these checksums, comments too, because the limits of the goals are stated in comments.
  - Six further files under Ladder/ are outside the twenty-nine and are not pinned. Five of them state no goal (BitsOfStep, FilesReadLaws, FitBindsBits, ReadThru, RequestResultTies: proofs, ties, or code beneath `verify`) [read]; two of these files' own opening comments say so.
  - The sixth, Ladder/FitBinds.lean, lies on the goals' side of the line for binding of the commitment (in the opening form), for no two openings and for correctness of extraction. It defines the error term `epsBind` (Ladder/FitBinds.lean:209), which the conclusions of some theorems on these name: so `extracts_atOlder` (Johnson/Goal/Conditions/BindsAtSecondHashFunction.lean:391-395), whose figure of about 2^-155 on this page is `epsBind` worked out, and Johnson/Goal/TopOfThreeBinding.lean:67. The two forms `soundAndBinding_level_digits_of_verify_at` and `soundOpened_level_digits_of_verify_at` (55) end in another term, `levelError`, whose file is pinned (the first bullet). No theorem on this page is graded against Ladder/FitBinds.lean. What the full check's step on imports admits there: 81 (d).
- **Layer.** The protocol as written in Lean: how its statements are pinned.
- **Why it matters.** A change of a goal's text fails this comparison unless the stored checksum in scripts/check.py is changed with it (scripts/check.py): a pass shows that the twenty-nine files are those that this script names, not that they were never changed. A change beneath the goals does not fail it: a definition of the library that a goal names can change, and the goal's meaning with it, without a failure here (scripts/check.py).

### 42 · What the goals as typed leave out

- **The limit.** The goals' straightline extractor sees neither the files' digest nor what the further public reading (`readMore`, the second of the two reads of the public files) returns, on which the compiler's extractor depends (Ladder.lean:365-369).
  - `FilesRead` states only the policy's three numbers of what the reader returns. The scale words, which make the model of every goal, are stated by no goal and evaluated by no guard (Ladder.lean:601-608; Ladder/Guards.lean:29-32).
  - Two of the goals' own comments are not kept to the letter (56, 65).
  - These lie in the goals as typed, that is in the files that state them (50), not in a proof beneath them; whether the goals can stand as typed is not known.
- **Layer.** The protocol as written in Lean.
- **Proved nearest to it.** That at both named sets the scale words are the reference table's is a theorem beside the goals (`VerifierAndGoals.read_full_ref`, `VerifierAndGoals.read_gap_ref`, Johnson/Goal/Files/FilesReadAtRef.lean:122, 132), of the bytes written in Ladder/Stored.lean.

### 80 · No system is refuted in Lean against the text of `Sound`

- **The limit.** No theorem of the library refutes a system against the text of `Sound`, `SoundForWith`, `ZeroKnowledge` or `ZeroKnowledgeOn`. The system that accepts everything has `acceptAll_complete` (VerifiableInference/Checks.lean:198) and no theorem that it is not sound. Three of the six top statements have conditional theorems against them at small error, each a bound on the error: `Complete` at every α < 1, of any system that rejects at a request whose output is the model's (`not_complete_of_rejected`, VerifiableInference/NotComplete.lean:36); `SoundOpened` at every error below 1 − α, and `SoundAndBinding` at every error below (1 − α)/2, of any system whose honest side is accepted but for α at a claim that some output not the model's meets (`one_sub_le_of_completeFor_soundOpened`, Ladder/PackageTies.lean:399; `soundOpened_of_soundAndBinding`, VerifiableInference/Checks.lean:48: `SoundAndBinding` at an error ε gives `SoundOpened` at 2 ε; under G3'pol, `packageSoundOpened_bar`, `packageSoundAndBinding_bar`; 39). All of this: [counted: a search of the files' text by pattern, which can miss a theorem].
- **Layer.** The protocol as written in Lean: its statements.
- **Why it matters.** `Sound` is the statement of the soundness theorem (28). Such a refutation would be a test that the definition of `Sound` does not let a wrong protocol pass; for the text of `Sound` Lean has none. It affects no proof; it bears on what the statement of (28) means.
- **Proved nearest to it.**
  - Against three definitions of VerifiableInference/MoreStatements.lean, of a system whose committer does not read the weights: `BindsWith`, `NoTwoOpenings` and `ExtractsCommittedWeights`, each at one stated error, at public files that are read (`blindTwin_fails`, Johnson/Bind/Cpp/Controls.lean:346; 72). None of the three is one of the four texts above.
  - Conditionally, against the text of `SoundOpened`, for every system: where the honest side is accepted, but for α, at an output that is not the model's, `SoundOpened` can hold only at an error of at least 1 − α at the honest side's number of queries (`one_sub_le_of_completeFor_soundOpened`, Ladder/PackageTies.lean:399). It names no system.
  - Against three forms in which the library states the law of a part of the protocol, made-up members are refuted: three toy segments against the law of a segment's batching (`LinkSem.Sound`, Johnson/Desc/LinkLaw2.lean:592: `yesToAll_not_sound`, `forgetful_not_sound`, `wipes_not_sound`, :1187, :1193, :1199); a text that always answers, against the form of one batching's law (`SoundAt`, Johnson/Desc/K13/Law/Law.lean:87: `not_sound_yes_gl`, Johnson/Desc/K13/Law/Pin.lean:87); a toy sub-protocol against the law of a sub-protocol that hears (`HearSem.Sound`, Johnson/Desc/RiderLaw.lean:137: `twice_not_sound`, :500). These forms are other definitions than the four texts above.
  - The library also refutes protocols, nearly all made-up toys, against its general definitions. None of these refutations is stated against a definition of VerifiableInference/Statements.lean or VerifiableInference/MoreStatements.lean:
    - Against index binding (`ZKCP.HasIndexBinding`): `acceptAll_not_binding`, `blindExtractor_not_binding` (ZKCP/Sanity.lean:277, :307), `quiet_not_binding_yy` (ZKCP/GenSanity.lean:293). These test the notion of which `Sound` is a case (`sound_iff`, 28), not the text of `Sound`.
    - Against index privacy and related notions: `leakIndex_not_private`, `leakWitness_not_private` (ZKCP/Sanity.lean:222, :246), `unsalted_not_private` (ZKCP/HashToy.lean:643), `nosy_not_private` (ZKCP/GenSanity.lean:259), `leaky_not_strong` (ZKCP/Separation.lean:300).
    - Against two further forms of completeness: `adaptive_fails` (ZKCP/Adaptive41.lean:70), `sep_not_refined` (ZKCP/Sep41.lean:134).
    - Against honest-verifier zero knowledge of an interactive protocol: `leaky_fails`, `chatty_fails` (Hiop/WholeTest.lean:137, :222).
    - Against round-by-round knowledge soundness at an extractor that cannot work: `const_fails`, `lit_fails` (Ior/KnowTest.lean:165, :167), `blind_fails` (Cb/Ld/LdBaseTest.lean:160).
- **Known attack or counterexample.** None known against `Sound`. That a useless system meets it is a theorem (`rejectAll_sound`, VerifiableInference/Checks.lean:181: the system that rejects everything); completeness, soundness and zero knowledge together: 39, "The six top statements as written", (f).

### 3.2 Completeness, and what can be run

Its entries: 52 · 73 · 45 · 56 · 65 · 26 · 58 · 46 · 33 · 53 · 48 · 62.

### 52 · Completeness: proved of the honest part, in the random-oracle model; at the real hash function an accept is evaluated, not proved

- **The limit; proved in part.** That `verify` accepts honest proofs is proved in the random-oracle model, for the honest part written in Lean: at public files in use, at every well-formed request whose claimed output is the model's own result, `verify` rejects the Lean prover's proof with probability at most 2^-40 (`completeAtWellFormedRequests`, which is equivalent to the line `completeOnCanServe`; 39). At BLAKE2b no theorem shows an acceptance: there, that `verify` accepts is [evaluated]. Of the Lean verifier's own system, whose honest side is a constant one, completeness cannot hold (55), and the goal on completeness under a policy (G3'pol), which is asked of that system, is not proved. Beside that line Lean proves a reduction to thirteen named hypotheses and that the gate accepts at the written copies (73). What that does not give:
  - (a) `Sound` holds of a verifier that rejects everything. The same holds of the three round-by-round statements of (36), which a decision that never says yes meets, and of the pair `G8bits` with `G9bits` at `theWideDigits` (`policy_and_binding_digits`, Johnson/Goal/PolicyAndBinding.lean:147; (55)). At the stored files the soundness theorem (28) would be vacuous if the gate (`protocolDataWellFormed`) rejected them. That it accepts is proved at the written copy (73).
  - (b) No theorem ties the written copies to the files in examples/: 73 (k).
  - (c) Two more places at which `verify` could reject everything on the public files alone are settled by theorems. Further ones are named, not all are settled, and no theorem says that the list is complete: 73 (a).
  - (d) That the thirteen hypotheses can hold together is shown only at the two written copies, at idle provers, which stop at every verifier message, and at an error of at least 3, where no bound says anything (`AtFullSide.Home.lastFits_idle_named`, AtFullSide/Home/HomeAtNamed.lean:92-96). On the road of the thirteen hypotheses a bound below 1 follows where provers that run the offline and the online IOP satisfy them at a small error; whether the honest provers written in Lean do is stated by no theorem (Johnson/Goal/Conditions/HomeLast.lean:20-22). The line `completeOnCanServe`, and with it `completeAtWellFormedRequests`, does not go by that road (39). See also (55).
  - (e) One of the thirteen, `honest`, is asked at every well-formed request that is true under the files' policy at some weights, whether that request can be served or not. If the IOP's lookups admit fewer values than the integer model admits, it can hold there only at error terms that sum to at least 1 [paper] (Johnson/Goal/Conditions/HomeLast.lean:152-158; Johnson/Goal/Conditions/HomeEnd.lean, limit 2).
  - (f) The theorems on the link proof below that are stated at `homeR`, the compiler's input at given public files with two written place-holders, an all-zero digest and an empty second public reading (Johnson/Goal/Conditions/HomeAtWider.lean:102-104; all of them but `dec_at_D` and `link_yes`), take the public-files digest as one fixed value, all zeros, at which `verify` is not proved ever to be called (E2E/LinkLaidPrivacy.lean:71-72). In all of them it is the interactive verifier that accepts: that the compiled verifier, or `verify`, accepts is not proved (AtFullSide/E2E/LinkLaidHashedAccepted.lean:14-15). No theorem ties the link proof's honest prover to a program.
  - (g) The soundness lemmas of the sub-protocols stand on conditions, of which four have no code: in Lean, `decodes`, `shown`, `noColl` and `pubP`. These four were neither evaluated nor proved at the honest run.

- **Layer.** The protocol as written in Lean: completeness of `verify` and its hypotheses. The tie between Lean and the programs: (b) and (f)'s last sentence.
- **Why it matters.** A gap in completeness can only cost a visible rejection. It cannot make `verify` accept a false claim beyond the theorem's bound, at the theorem's conditions, provided the functions that form the claim are right. Whether `verify` ever accepts, the soundness theorem alone does not say (a); `completeAtWellFormedRequests` says it in the random-oracle model (39), and the runs at the real hash function, as far as they go (14).

### 73 · The theorem on the gate removes one cause of vacuity, and no more

- **The limit.** The gate accepts, by theorem, at the library's written copies of the stored sets, in a side library. That removes one cause for which the soundness theorem (28) could be vacuous there, and no more. It does not show that `verify` accepts any proof: to pass the gate is needed for an accept and is not enough, and one accepted proof would be less than completeness. What other theorems say of an accept at `VerifierAndGoals.storedPublicFiles`, in the random-oracle model: 52, 39.
  - (a) *The tests are not all.* The gate is not all at which `verify` can reject everything on the public files alone, and no theorem says that the list of such places is complete. The test of the scope is in front of the gate. Two reads of the files (`readConfig`, `readMore`: Ladder.lean:407-411) are outside it. The outer branches of the decision on the copy's file, which reads the parameter file a second time, are beside it; it refuses on that file only outside the goals' scope. Three places are settled side by side at these files (below): the scope's test, the gate itself and these outer branches. The third follows from the first at all files in scope, so they amount to two conditions. Further places [read in part: `verify` has not been read to the bottom for them]:
    - The plan is derived a second time, at the sampled hash function's digest of the files; that succeeds where the tests pass (`FlashProve.sevenRead_of_live`, Johnson/Goal/Reads/SevenAtLive.lean:87). Each decision asks its tests on the plan again at its start. A lemma settles that for the copy's file, and theorems settle it for the key file and the proof file at `VerifierAndGoals.storedPublicFiles` (`AtFullSide.Top.keyPlanOk_zFull`, AtFullSide/Top/PlanOkAtFull.lean:77; `AtFullSide.Top.proofPlanOk_zFull`, AtFullSide/Top/PlanOkAtFull.lean:57). At the other policies it is [read].
    - The key file's decision begins with a test on the instance that passes exactly where three public numbers of the plan are as typed. A lemma settles it in scope, under further hypotheses: the public part read, the plan derived, its tests passed (`Johnson.RunOne.Law.OkStartInScope.okStart_in_scope`, Johnson/RunOne/Law/OkStartInScope.lean:44). No known theorem applies it at `VerifierAndGoals.storedPublicFiles`.
    - The proof file's decision compares the request's item with what a second reader of the parameter file returns (`OnBytes.Model.Verify.statementHeld`, OnBytes/Model/VerifyStatement.lean:52). Its outcome depends on the public files and the request's length alone [read], and making the item can refuse too. At `VerifierAndGoals.storedPublicFiles` and at `VerifierAndGoals.zGap`, at every request string of the right length, the item is made and the comparison passes, at the second hash function (`AtFullSide.Top.item_zFull_whole`, AtFullSide/Top/ItemAtFull.lean:57; `AtFullSide.Top.item_zGap_whole`, AtFullSide/Top/ItemAtFull.lean:69; in the side library: (h)). That these are the arguments at which the proof file's decision asks it is left to the caller, by that file's opening comment (AtFullSide/Top/ItemAtFull.lean:24-25). What completeness at the stored files yields: the next point. That it passes inside `verify` is also inferred from runs in which the form accepted honest proofs [evaluated]; over which files: "Runs and counts".
    - The encoding of the commitment and the building of the instance can each refuse. This page names no theorem that says of either by itself that it passes at these files, at `verify`'s own input. At `VerifierAndGoals.storedPublicFiles` neither refuses everything, nor does any other place of this list, in the random-oracle model [paper]: at made-up weights, at every non-empty prompt in the vocabulary and every output length that fit a request, at the model's own output, `verify` rejects the Lean prover's proof with probability at most the completeness error (`complete_at_the_stored_files_at_the_constant_weights`, AtFullSide/E2E/CompleteAtTheStoredFilesAtSmallWeights.lean:109, in the side library: (h); 52; 39). At BLAKE2b, and at the other policies, no theorem says as much.
    - Inside the chains' decisions hundreds of further clauses read the public files alone, by a single count. Some are settled in scope by lemmas and some cannot fail; for the others one search met no lemma that settles them, which is an upper bound [read in part: the sub-protocols with rounds were only surveyed]. The theorems that the search met for these lead from a pass and not to it, or reach a pass only under hypotheses that are shown at no state which a chain reaches at `VerifierAndGoals.storedPublicFiles`. Many of the clauses have a counterpart among the tests on the plan that `protocolDataWellFormed` runs, and the search met no theorem that joins the two [read in part]. At the full-size example's files, which Lean's evaluator found to be `VerifierAndGoals.storedPublicFiles`'s three files byte for byte, all of them passed at the reference run, since an honest proof was accepted there [evaluated]; over which files it was made: "Runs and counts".

A pass says that the plan is derived; that the offline and the online IOP are laid out, each with tests of its own, at one depth; that a test on numbers passes at 2^20 queries; and that none of the six lists of rounds is empty. No decision of the protocol is run: the theorem would stand unchanged if every sub-protocol's decision rejected everything, or accepted everything, so long as the tests on the plan stay.
