  - Many closed facts beneath the theorem are proved by evaluation in Lean's kernel (`decide +kernel`; the layout file's literal by `kernel_rfl`): no natively compiled code is trusted for them. Lean's output of axioms for the named theorems shows no axiom beyond Lean's three standard ones (18) [tested].
- **Known attack or counterexample.** None known in what the theorem says.

### 45 · What runs from a shell is the executable form of `verify`, evaluated inside Lean: the form is proved sound, a run of it is not

- **The limit.** What runs from a shell is the executable form of `verify`, inside Lean (`tools/lean-verify/`). Where the hash function's answers have 32 bytes, as the real one's have, it is proved equal to `verify` and proved sound at the same bound (56). No program is proved sound as it runs: what is not proved is what lies around the form: what a run trusts (a), the nine files under `tools/lean-verify/` (b), and the functions that form the claim (37). This folder's build file makes no executable. The verifier that users run by default is the C++ verifier, of which nothing is proved (section 1).
  - (a) *What a run trusts.* A run of the command is an evaluation. It trusts Lean's evaluator, compiler and runtime, the natively compiled helper library and the C compiler that builds it, and the file that drives it.
  - (b) *What ships.* Nine files under `tools/lean-verify/`: the command `lean_verify.sh`; `LeanVerify.lean`, which starts a run; `read_claim.py`, a second reader of the claim, in Python (37); `native-modules.txt`, the list of modules compiled to native code, with `LeanVerifyWalk.lean`, which generates it, and `check_list.sh`, which regenerates it and compares; two tests of the scripts, one with substitutes for Lean and the parser and one that compares the second reader with what `cp_verify` printed for the stored examples; and README.md. No theorem is about any of the nine, and neither `scripts/check.py` nor the second pass of Lean's kernel covers them. Johnson/PVR/Program.lean, ProgramTotal.lean and ProgramGuard.lean belong to the Lean library and are built and checked with it.
  - (c) *What a build of the command checks.* No theorem: a build audits neither `sorry` nor axioms. It does compile ProgramTotal.lean's theorems and ProgramGuard.lean's checks, and without them the command gives no verdict. What checks the theorems: CHECKING.md.
  - (d) *What cannot be repeated from this repository.* Most runs on this page were made over files of the library and sources of the programs that differ from this repository's. They gave `verify` the parsed form of the prover's files, and part of each ran as natively compiled code. Apart from the command's runs they were driven by files of `#eval` commands, which also made the alterations, in memory, with a natively compiled helper library; neither is in this repository, and neither has been independently reviewed.

    The executable form's file ships (Johnson/PVR/RideThe.lean). Some runs went through other files, not in this repository, of which it is a port, and the tie from such a run's output to the theorems is by name and statement, not by bytes (Johnson/PVR/RideThe.lean:18-24; (14)).

    Apart from the stored proofs, all runs went on proof files that are not in this repository, on synthetic weights, some made by a test prover (14, 76). The runs on altered parsed files (14, 66) cannot be made with this command: it takes the prover's own files and parses them itself.
  - (e) *Not tried.* Lean as installed by step 2 of CHECKING.md, with the C compiler bundled in Lean's official release (the runs had Lean pre-installed, with gcc 15.3.0). The packages as downloaded by step 3 of CHECKING.md (the runs used a copy compiled beforehand: 81 (g)). The option `--no-native` through this script. Any system but Linux x86-64. `scripts/check.py`, a whole build or the test suite after the command has written its compiled objects into lean/.lake.
  - (f) *What the answer looks like.* The answer is the exit code and one line on standard output. An accept's line names the policy, by the parameter file's lines C, theta and cap: `VERDICT: ACCEPT (exact match)` where they read 256, 256 and 0 (and sh and sT 8 and 4), and else `VERDICT: ACCEPT (under the files' policy C=.. theta=.. cap=..; NOT exact match: see "What an accept means")`, with the three numbers (44). That heading is in tools/lean-verify/README.md. On the small examples the command prints `NO VERDICT`, since it takes full-size files alone; that is no finding against those proofs.
- **Layer.** The tie between Lean and the programs: between the term of the theorem, its executable form and the command.
- **Why it matters.** Somebody who runs the command relies on (56) for what its verdict means, and on (a), on the nine files of (b) and on (37) for the verdict itself.
- **Proved nearest to it.** The form's verdict, on what its own parser reads, is `verify`'s, and the form always gives one where the hash function's answers have 32 bytes and is sound at the same bound: `PV.Ride.theForm_eq_verify`, Johnson/PVR/SoundOfTheForm.lean:87-91; `PV.Ride.verdictThe_some`, Johnson/PVR/RideEveryHash.lean:73-75; `PV.Ride.sound_of_the_form_events`, Johnson/PVR/SoundOfTheFormEvents.lean:252-257 (56).
- **Tested nearest to it.**
  - *What a reader can try,* with this repository's files and two small files that the reader types. In the reference run the command gave: the accept of the stored full-size proof (examples/full-size/; how it was made: its README.md); a reject of the same proof under another commitment ID; a reject of it under another last token of the output [evaluated]. The list of natively compiled modules (`native-modules.txt`: (b)) can be made anew [tested]. At the second stored set (examples/full-size-clipped-gap/), whose policy is not exact match (44), the command accepted the stored proof and printed the second line of (f), with C=25856 theta=512 cap=1 [evaluated]. An accept there is of a stored proof, not of a proof that the reader's own build of the prover made (14).
  - The cost comes first: Lean and its packages, a long build of a part of the library, then a run that is long unless it ends early (tools/lean-verify/README.md).
  - The command takes any proof in the format's default setting at full size, so also one that a reader makes with the prover in this repository, which needs a GPU. What has been put to it, and under which conditions: (14).

### 56 · The term `verify` cannot be executed as typed

- **The limit.** Parts of the term `verify` are defined by the axiom of choice, among them the one-to-one coding of its hash queries (Johnson/Top/Fire/Code/Dec/MOneD.lean:129-131), so it cannot be executed as typed. What runs is its executable form, which is proved equal to `verify` at every hash function with 32-byte answers and is itself proved sound at the same bound (below). What that does not cover:
  - (a) Point 9 of the opening comment of Ladder.lean forbids choice in the fields that run (`C`, `F`, `P`, `readMore`, `T`, `parse`, `sample`, `commit`, `prove`) and allows it to the extractor (Ladder.lean:135-136). The term does not keep to that rule. The uses of choice beneath `verify` are ten [counted]. Three are in the extractor alone, which point 9 allows. Four are in the code's decoder and the configuration, in the types of parameters. Two are in the coding of the hash queries (below). The tenth is Lean's own: a well-foundedness check on a loop over string iterators in Lean's core library (46).
    - Of the two in the coding, one is the parser of the coding's inverse (OnBytes/TypedOracleCompiler/Run/PageOf.lean:56-61), where the chosen thing is unique since the coding is proved one to one; of the other, uniqueness is not proved. The first query of the copy's chain is spelled through a chosen inverse, and which string is asked is held by a theorem, not by the text (OnBytes/TypedOracleCompiler/Run/LinkFirstAns.lean:52, OnBytes/TypedOracleCompiler/Run/LinkFirstAns.lean:91).
  - (b) Lean's mark `noncomputable` is on the IOP's value, on `verify`, on `systemOfRecord`, on the coding and on the IOP's proof parser. So point 9 is kept neither at `T` nor at `parse`, nor at `C` and `F` [counted]. The structure that declares these fields is in the files that state the goals (50).
  - (c) The theorems below are statements about the term's value, not about whether or when an evaluation ends. What a run trusts beside them: 45 (a).
- **Layer.** The protocol as written in Lean: how the term `verify` is defined, and the goals' rule for the fields that run. The tie between Lean and the programs: the executable form.
- **Why it matters.** The goals' own rule for the fields that run is not kept to the letter. This is no fault of soundness [read]. At every hash function whatever verdict the executable form gives is `verify`'s, so choice decides nothing there.
- **Proved nearest to it.**
  - `PV.Ride.theForm_eq_verify` (Johnson/PVR/SoundOfTheForm.lean:87-91): at every function whose answers have 32 bytes, at all public files and on every parsed proof file, the form's verdict is `verify`'s decision.
  - `PV.Ride.sound_of_the_form_events` (Johnson/PVR/SoundOfTheFormEvents.lean:252-257): a soundness statement of its own, `PV.Ride.SoundOfDecision`, of the form's decision, with the hash function drawn at random, at the same bound and under the same three hypotheses: files in scope, files read, exact match.
  - `PV.Ride.verdictThe_some` (Johnson/PVR/RideEveryHash.lean:73-75): the form always gives a verdict where the function's answers have 32 bytes.
  - `verdictThe_is_theDescription` (Johnson/PVR/RideThe.lean:132-138): a verdict that the form gives is `verify`'s decision, at every function from byte strings to byte strings and at any public files, on one condition: that the form gives a verdict at all. `forall_hashOf32` (Johnson/Goal/Tie/HashOfEvery.lean:31-34): every hash function of the goals' model is given by such a function, one with 32-byte answers. So whatever verdict the form gives is `verify`'s, at every hash function. Both, asked by name, rest on no axiom beyond Lean's three standard ones (18) [tested].
- **Tested nearest to it.** That no code which a run of the executable form executes calls the axiom of choice is not a theorem. It rests on two things; a third checks a part of what the walks rely on.
  - The executable form is computable: Lean compiles it, natively in part, and does not mark it noncomputable [read], and it has run [evaluated]. So, as far as Lean's evaluator, compiler and runtime are trusted, no code that a run executes calls choice.
  - All of this bullet: [counted]. From the form's code (`PV.Ride.verdictThe`, `rideOf`, `letsThe`), through definitions and never into a proof, which the compiler erases, none of the library's own definitions names the axiom of choice or is marked noncomputable. The only definitions met that name it are two of Lean's own core library (`Classical.propDecidable`, `Classical.ofNonempty`), and none of the library's definitions that were met names either. That is the tenth use above [counted].

    Two walks from the form's code, through definitions and never into a proof, find none of the library's own constants marked noncomputable, each with a control that gives a finding at `VerifierAndGoals.verify`. The first, with Lean's and Mathlib's constants opened and theorems not opened, met 14,224 of the library's own constants that are no theorems: none names `Classical.choice`, and, as it alone of the two looked for, none is replaced by other code when compiled (`implemented_by`, `extern`). (The first walk also meets seven constants of Lean's own library that Lean marks noncomputable, `List.filterMap`, `Nat.modCore` and `WellFounded.fix` among them; 36 of the library's own constants name `List.filterMap`.) [counted]

    The other, which did not open Lean's, Std's or Mathlib's libraries and looked for a list of 41 names of the choice family, met 15,122, and none names that family. Through the proofs too it met 16,954: there 26 definitions are marked noncomputable and 31 constants (3 definitions, 28 theorems) name that family [counted].
  - A standing check runs whenever the library is built, and the build fails if it fails: `#guard_cone` walks all constants that `PV.Program.run` uses, by the kernel's terms, and lists those whose compiled code is not made from the definition that the kernel checked (`implemented_by`, `extern` and the other kinds of OnBytes/Guard/Cone.lean:7-15). It fails if this library's group contains any (Johnson/PVR/ProgramGuard.lean:8-11). It does not look for the axiom of choice, nor for Lean's mark noncomputable: an axiom of Lean's core that occurs in the cone is listed in the first group and not judged (OnBytes/Guard/Cone.lean:63-74, OnBytes/Guard/Cone.lean:129-143). What it checks here is that none of the library's own constants in that cone runs other code than its definition's, by one of these kinds.

    `#guard_has` checks that the parts that matter occur in that cone: the reader of the claim, the executable form, the format's hash (Johnson/PVR/ProgramGuard.lean:16-17) [counted: at every build].

    In another build that cone contained 17,738 constants; listed were 179 of Lean's core and Std, 5 of Mathlib and the packages, and none of this library [counted]. The first two groups are listed and not judged; the rules for the compiler by which a constant is replaced are not checked there; the walk follows the kernel's terms, not the compiler's output, so it lists too much rather than too little; the three lines of tools/lean-verify/LeanVerify.lean lie outside it; and nothing there speaks of Lean's compiler, interpreter or runtime (Johnson/PVR/ProgramGuard.lean:11-15, Johnson/PVR/ProgramGuard.lean:24-28). Further limits: OnBytes/Guard/Cone.lean:38-46.
  - Beside this: as far as Lean's evaluator, compiler and runtime are trusted, each accept in a run is that of `verify` in the state of the files over which it runs, at the real hash function. So, as far as the runs told here go, `verify` as it stood at those calls did not reject everything (14; 26; over which files: "Runs and counts").

### 65 · No run can be a run of the term itself

- **The limit.** The comment on `verify` says that a run counts for the final statement only if it runs the term there (Ladder.lean:414-415). None can, since the term cannot be executed as typed (56). With point 9 (56) this is the second place where the goals' own comments are not kept to the letter.
- **Layer.** The tie between Lean and the programs.
- **Why it matters.** An accept told of `verify` is its executable form's, and `verify`'s by the tie (56).

### 26 · Runs over other states of the files are tied to one another by checksums over `verify`'s text; the tie to this repository's `verify` is not held (section 6)

- **The limit.** Many of the runs told on this page are over other states of the library's files. Within one state, the form is tied to that state's `verify` by a theorem (56); that a run executed the form: (b), 45 (d). What ties those states to one another is checksums over the text of `verify` [counted], and no theorem. These checksums are not taken over the files of this repository, and the definitions beneath `verify` differ from those states': the tie to this repository's `verify` is not held (section 6; the reference run, the calls on the 41 further proofs and, as far as that paragraph tells, the call on the one proof more (14) are not among these: "Runs and counts"). A run from this repository (45) does not need this tie.
  - (a) No list in this repository fixes by name the small test functions of `verify-on-bytes` that `verify` calls.
  - (b) The functions that drive a run are on no fixed list. The evaluation of the fourteen entry tests (14, *The fourteen entry tests*) went through a copy of the form's file (Johnson/PVR/RideThe.lean), not that file. Every item of that file occurs letter for letter in the copy, which contains more [counted], and no theorem says that the two are equal. No build from scratch and no re-check by the kernel stood beneath it. Two further trials called the file itself, with thirteen entry tests, not fourteen: the honest proof was accepted, and the proof file with one bit altered was rejected [evaluated].
  - (c) The text of `verify` is the same across those states but for the tests on the public files in front of it: one of those states has eight entry tests, not fourteen [counted].
  - (d) That two states of the files are the same in code is a comparison by lexers and comparers written for it, which are not Lean's parser [counted: once]; where names have changed, a comparer cuts both texts into tokens and compares each changed name, part by part, with tables of the names before and after, the files being paired by a list of moved paths: it shows that nothing but names changed, not that a name means the same declaration at each place. A documentation comment counts as a comment there, and Lean keeps it in the compiled file. Such a comparison is good against a slip, not against intent.
- **Layer.** The tie between Lean and the programs: between the term of the theorem and what ran.
- **Why it matters.** Without this tie a run over another state of the files and the laws would be about different objects.
- **Tested nearest to it.** All of this field: [counted].
  - A walk inside Lean takes checksums over the text of `verify`: 1,848 constants at the top, 18,776 beneath, and 572 in the entry tests' part. Over other states of the Lean files it gives the same two checksums each time, and for the entry tests' part the checksum of a state on which a run at full size was made. Over another build, again of Lean files that differ from this repository's, the walk gives the same checksums: all nine running checksums that were asked came out as expected, among them, by the walk's own labels, the two over the text of `verify` (b428efe6eef697cea39741f1268d8994c0376ed5b4ce77ce571b28417b319897 at the top, f909f9fb032a2517c11f6d9484bc610c02dadb26d9df2090b074bd705406ef4a beneath) and the one over the entry tests' part (66a959e841478347a4d0b7c4df8c71599f961e54492cd77cee2d440005b71201). One part of the walk has a second form, over ten more constants (5,817 against 5,807); its two checksums were not asked, because this form imports a file that is not in this repository.
  - These are counts of lists, not parts of one total: 1,692 of the top's 1,848 constants occur in the list beneath too, and a fourth list has 431. A walk with nothing excluded counts 19,818 constants, each in one of the four lists.
- **Known attack or counterexample.** None known.

### 58 · Two texts, and one is never run

- **The limit.** The term of which the soundness theorem (28) speaks carries the engine of the first chain of `verify-on-bytes` as data: at least 306 of the 7,987 definitions that the statement unfolds to, in 29 modules under OnBytes/Model/ (Tail, Hide, Layer, Look, Trees, Head and others) [counted]. They occur in a field `run` of a link's structure (filled at OnBytes/Model/VerifyLinks.lean:25-45) and in one more field, which no text applies. What decides are copies under Johnson/Desc/, which by their own comments are typed after them line for line.
  - (a) Of the same kind: the format's verifier under Fmt/ is not on the path of `verify`; of the reply's step, a test and its 28 guard lines guard no text that runs; `Request.ok` and `Reply.fin` do not run [counted].
  - (b) Comments in 66 files under OnBytes/TypedOracleCompiler/ call what lies there a second form of the verifier's side of the library's compiler, or of its road to soundness [counted: a search for the words "second form of"]. These modules contain the compiler that the soundness theorem is about.
- **Layer.** The protocol as written in Lean: which of its texts decides.
- **Why it matters.** It is harmless for soundness. But whoever audits OnBytes/Model/Tail.lean or OnBytes/Model/Head.lean as what `verify` tests reads the text that is carried as data, not the text that decides.

### 46 · What is trusted when compiled Lean code runs

- **The limit.** In compiled Lean code (Lean v4.29.1) the sorts run through `@[csimp]` replacements, each justified by a proved equation. The string primitives beneath `String.splitOn` are `@[extern]`: C code of Lean's runtime, trusted and not proved. Beneath `String.toNat?` a well-founded fixpoint is `@[implemented_by]`, with no proved equation. This is so everywhere in Lean, not in this library alone.
- **Layer.** The tie between Lean and the programs: here, between a Lean definition and its compiled code.
- **Why it matters.** It affects no theorem. It bears on every run of compiled Lean code (14, 33, 52).

### 33 · The negative tests of the sub-protocols run the decisions, not `verify`

- **The limit.** The negative tests of the sub-protocols run the decisions of the protocol as Lean describes it, not `verify`. Most ran at the small size, whose files `verify` rejects. All ran on synthetic weights.
- **Layer.** The protocol as written in Lean: these are tests of its decisions, not of the programs.
- **Why it matters.** These are tests, not proofs, and they say of `verify` only what is told above. What the theorems written as negative tests inside Lean show: (53).
- **Tested nearest to it.**
  - *Small size.* 1,265 tests were run once: 1,225 rejected and 35 accepted, each as expected (the 35 are changes of the IOP's text that should not matter); 2 void; 3 with no expected verdict (two accepted: a weakened IOP on the honest proof; one rejected) [evaluated]. Altered values, items, whole rounds, counts, top rows and sampled values, in a prover's or a user's hand: 2,146 rejected, none accepted; 120 changes of the IOP's text at the same places: rejected too; 86 void; 23 not run [evaluated].
  - *Full size.* 217 altered values and words of opened columns were rejected, none accepted. 1,104 negative tests were run once: 1,066 rejected and 33 accepted, each as expected, 2 void, and 3 with no expected verdict as at the small size. The 920 tests run at both sizes gave the same verdict at both [evaluated]. Which test was expected to be rejected and which accepted was listed by name only after the first run had begun; only the totals stood before it.
  - *A false copy's file.* A well-formed false copy's file of a cheating committer, at the full size, was rejected through a run of the executable form of `verify`, by that file's own decision, which was reached (key file: accept; copy's file: reject; proof file: accept). Its honest counterpart was accepted. The C++ verifier rejects the same cases [tested]. Which test inside the decision rejected, the run's output does not show [evaluated]. The files are not in this repository.
  - *One sent value of a lookup stretch altered by one.* The sub-protocol alone still accepts. The whole run rejected 30 of 30 at the small size, 50 of 50 at the full size and 20 of 20 further ones at the small size, at the first chain's proximity test. This was at the test harness's views, not on an altered proof file [evaluated].

### 53 · Most theorems written as negative tests, where sorted, are no evidence that the tests can fail

- **The limit.** Most of the theorems written as negative tests, where they have been sorted, do not show that the test can say no. Each is still a true theorem that Lean's kernel has checked, and no statement on soundness turns on this. A negative test, here, is a theorem that says: at a changed value this test of the library says no. The evidence is weaker than the files' comments tell.
  - (a) Of the 236 shipped theorems written as negative tests of the proximity test (170) and of attention's own rounds (66, eight of them in the masked sumcheck's folder), 23 show something: five are controls, which come to three facts that only running a routine at the changed value tells, and 18 show little. At 29 nothing is shown as they stand: a mere refusal is not ruled out, or no line shows that the premises can be met. 183 show nothing. One changes nothing [counted] [read]. No Lean was run for this sorting.
  - (b) Of the 183, 65 cannot fail whatever the data (the test asks e < d, and the theorem gives it e := d), 57 are said already by other theorems and would fail only together with them, and 61 would fail only if written data were other. What a theorem of the second sort says is stated in the library, in a positive theorem, and the count does not say that nothing in the library tries these routines at bad values.
  - (c) 153 of the 183 are called a test, a control or "can fail" in their comment, their section's heading or their file's opening comment: for instance `noChain_nil`, Johnson/Desc/K12/Coin/LawAtLight.lean:84; `k12AllB_nil`, `k12AllB_twice`, Johnson/Loose/H2K12/K12LawR.lean:87, Johnson/Loose/H2K12/K12LawR.lean:90; `k2W_groups_no`, Johnson/Loose/H2K12/SpotsAtAll.lean:272; and 32 in the opening comment of Johnson/Desc/K12/Law/RoundsHoldPlaces.lean.
  - (d) The count is a floor: not every negative test of these two kinds is caught, and those of the other sub-protocols are not counted. Two theorems elsewhere also cannot fail (`frontB_noReads`, Johnson/Road/K4/K4RowTestLaws.lean:235; `batch_place_bent`, Johnson/P5J/K09LeftCellsPub.lean:52). Whether these are among those counted here, and whether any of these theorems are among the totals of (33), is not known.
  - (e) Of one set of 70 files whose `#guard` commands the full check lists, 30 have no negative test of their own; each command of these 70 files can fail by its text. Johnson/Goal/Meaning/ScopeRun.lean has no negative test: each of its guards expects a yes (Johnson/Goal/Meaning/ScopeRun.lean:58-72), so here its test is never shown to say no.

    By four opening comments (Johnson/Road/Ends/GuardRec.lean, Johnson/Road/FormsKeysRunP.lean, Johnson/Road/HeadRunK.lean, Johnson/Road/Lane5KvWit.lean) a control on `tablesB`, the guard on P6, the comparison of the tables (`runHT`) and three further premises of the seam are not run in this library: these are not shown here (LIMITS.md, L56). By a search of the file names, at several hundred places the library's comments name a Lean file by a path at which this repository has no file, and do not say so (README.md) [counted].

    One command of Johnson/Road/Rows/TestAttPlaces.lean tests four tables alone: at the others its right side is the left side's own term. All of (e) but the search's count: [read: once].
- **Layer.** The protocol as written in Lean: theorems in the library that are meant as tests.
- **Why it matters.** These theorems are meant as tests: they are no proof that the tests can say no. This gap affects no statement on soundness. It bears only on how far these theorems show that certain tests can say no.

### 48 · The fourteenth entry test compares four of a round's seven fields

- **The limit.** Entry test 14 derives every chain's plan a second time and compares it with the planned one in four of a round's seven fields (parameter entry, error bound, kind of argument, scope). It does not compare the group from which the error was computed, the bound from which the count in its closeness error comes (`rests`), or the flag which says that the word on how the round's error was computed contradicts the round (`wordFault`; OnBytes/Params/Planner.lean:95-109). It also asks that a decoding radius can be computed for each sampled oracle message and, in the first proof chain alone, that one shape of oracle message has one radius (Johnson/Desc/Laid/ProofAsks.lean:58-61). It is not known to reject any public files that tests 1 to 13 let through (Johnson/Desc/Laid/ProofAsks.lean:64); at altered Lean values, which are no files, it has rejected alone (14, *The fourteen entry tests*). It stands for a condition that the proofs need and that is not proved for all files in scope (28). Its words of rejection, a string in the code, say more than the test compares: of the rounds and the oracle messages they say that these cannot be derived again from the sizes (Johnson/Desc/Laid/ProofAsks.lean:128-130). The file's opening comment tells what the test compares (Johnson/Desc/Laid/ProofAsks.lean:54-58).
- **Layer.** The protocol as written in Lean.
- **Why it matters.** Whoever reads the rejection message takes the test for more than it compares. No theorem depends on more than the test says [read].

### 62 · That every lawful scale file passes the plan's tests has not been compared

- **The limit.** The plan's tests are made on the scale file and on the eight numbers that the scope leaves free (35), in several places. Whether every file that the goals call lawful (35) passes them has not been compared.
- **Layer.** The protocol as written in Lean.
- **Why it matters.** It is a limit of completeness, beside (57) and (52).

### 3.3 What the proof rests on, and how it is checked

Its entries: 12 · 18 · 27 · 49 · 61 · 63 · 79 · 81.

### 12 · Coding theory

- **The limit.** That the Lean statement of MCA for Reed–Solomon codes up to (just inside) the Johnson radius (`Cb.BCHKS46At`, Cb/Gen/CodeListRSAt.lean:88) follows from the published theorem (Theorem 4.6 of ePrint 2025/2055), from its case of two words, is [read]. That it is the bound that a technical report on this protocol that is not public (16) uses at this project's parameters is [paper].
  - The proof of that statement rests on 140 of the 141 files under ThirdParty/ (by imports), 139 of which AI systems adapted from ArkLib and Mathlib (LIMITS.md, L15; which file follows which source: CREDITS.md, CREDITS-FILES.tsv).
  - That the statement is proved here says nothing on whether the published theorem is formally verified: the library's files that prove the statement do not prove the paper's own form of the bound. The published theorem stands in the full version alone (ePrint 2025/2055); the proceedings state the plain form.
  - The soundness theorem's statement shows no trace of this dependence, and Lean's list of axioms does not show it, since the statement on codes is a theorem and no axiom. It is found at the calls of `Cb.Bridge.foldErr_ix` (Johnson/Bridge/FoldErrIx.lean:51) and `Cb.Gl.bchks46At` (Cb/Bridge/GlCodesProved.lean:20).
  - The soundness rests on list decoding up to (just inside) the Johnson radius [read] [counted]. At all 181 written pairs of shape and radius of the proof file the radius is at least half the distance (where a stage's rows lie in two codes, the smaller of their two distances) [paper] (LIMITS.md, L81).
  - *From two words to many rows* (38). The stored parameter file has `rowcost 1` (Ladder/Stored.lean:36): combining the rows of an interleaved word costs no more than a line through two words. That the soundness theorem derives what it needs through `rows_free_abs` and `ldFc_mca_prox`, and that its proof term reaches these two, is not known. The kernel has checked the theorem whichever lemmas it goes through.
  - *The fit is sharp.* At the 59 rounds of the shared chain's two proximity tests, which a batch shares, the least room under the bound on a round's error (about 2^-128: 36 (a)) is 0.009 bit, and one more in a radius breaks it. A folding round of one kind is at 0.99 of the limit [paper].
  - *What is beneath the soundness theorem.* This statement and at most 1,429 constants under ThirdParty/ are beneath theorems that the soundness theorem calls [counted]. Six of the 146 called theorems depend on ThirdParty/, where a called theorem is one named by a theorem of the folder Johnson/Goal/Skeleton/ and lying outside it: `P5J.k10_kind_free`, `H2K12.k12_kind_at_head`, `VerifierAndGoals.AtTheName.keySound_at_live_holds`, `P5J.five_at_free2_laid2`, `P5J.AtMgA.lastLeft_at_head_alone` and `FlashProve.entrySound` [counted].
- **Layer.** The protocol as written in Lean.
- **Why it matters.** The soundness theorem (28) rests on this statement. Lean's kernel checks what these files yield; that the Lean statement is the published one is a separate matter, told above. These files are one of the three places where a fault would most likely hide, beside a definition inside the statement and the library's own code that runs at build time (18).
- **Proved nearest to it.** The statement is a theorem of this library at its two fields, and Lean accepts its proof: `Cb.Gl.bchks46At` (Cb/Bridge/GlCodesProved.lean:20), statement at Cb/Gen/CodeListRSAt.lean:88. The library states the step from two words to many rows in an abstract form, as linear algebra over a finite field (statement: `rows_free_abs`, Cb/Ld/LdRelRowsEAbs.lean:90-99), and proves it (Cb/Ld/LdRelRowsEAbsProofs.lean:89). `ldFc_mca_prox` is at Cb/Ld/LdFoldEqCode.lean:116 (statement) and Cb/Ld/LdFoldEqCodeProofs.lean:55 (proof).
- **Known attack or counterexample.** None known.

### 18 · The checking itself

- **The limit.** No single check covers everything, and none replaces another. What the script of the full check leaves unchecked: (81). No check sees what a file's text means behind its notations and macros, or a definition beneath the goals, which is not pinned. The independent checker (nanoda) gave no verdict at four theorems, and the wider passes told at (b), over Lean files that differ from this repository's, in code too, ended without a verdict for many more (why, as far as the record says, is told there) and refused nothing; no run shows it refusing a wrong proof.

  One whole run of the library's full check (`scripts/check.py`) over the Lean library in `lean/` has the last line "All steps passed: tree, words, imports, build (kernel replay, audit). …", at the checksum c06b37e6bf53ef5577cdb397c9835c363eef30d5753ce514659c18d1b34da87a (what `python3 -B scripts/check.py --digest` printed over the files of that run; with the file that was added afterwards and the lists as extended ("Runs and counts") it prints 620d8930cb09859399d9a75a15878550ee98c6a4b57753f560e33a04c515f5f5). That line also says that the result is about the compiled definitions ("read the listing, not the source"), that at least 7,888 declarations program Lean in the modules below one named theorem ((c)), and that no theorem got a verdict on binders or on `Classical.choice`: every line of `scripts/top-theorems.txt` says "general" (the whole line: [CHECK-RESULTS.md](CHECK-RESULTS.md)). It is one run, on one machine. In its kernel replay 1,438 modules go by another call than `leanchecker`; Mathlib and the other packages are taken as they were compiled beforehand, and are not replayed (81 (g)); the 13 files under `Ask/` are read by the text steps alone. The script runs no mutation tests and no second, independent proof checker. The last column tells that run [tested].

  - (a) *A successful build proves little by itself.* A Lean file can build with exit code 0 and no error line while a theorem in it rests on a step left unproved (`sorryAx` among its axioms). It gives no verdict on them (its lines begin "syntax, no verdict"), and its counts are lower bounds: a `run_cmd` can change how a file is read and leave nothing in the compiled file (scripts/RULES.txt, "What the audit does about this class"). - **Layer.** The protocol as written in Lean: how the Lean proofs are checked.
- **Why it matters.** Lean's kernel checks whatever the library's own code makes, so none of it can make a theorem false. But what a file's text seems to say can depend on a notation, a macro or an instance above it. The library's own tactics and elaborators that run at build time are one of the three places where a fault would most likely hide, beside the files on coding theory (12) and a definition inside the statement.

### 27 · The laws speak only where their conditions hold, which is evaluated in part, not proved

- **The limit.** A law is the soundness lemma of one sub-protocol. It speaks only at a statement inside its standing conditions, the conditions on that statement that it assumes, at which its own sub-protocol accepts. That such a place exists at the stored sets is not proved, and is evaluated in part alone.
  - On the proof file's first chain seven standing conditions that have code were evaluated along one honest full-size run, and the four that have no code were neither evaluated nor proved (52).
  - For the key file's 21 laws there is one example each, at oracles and transcripts constructed for the evaluation (section 5, (2)).
  - For none of them does Lean's kernel prove an example at this repository's values.
  - Laws also take bundled premises, named conditions that are not tests of the verifier. Values are shown for one bundle, `P5J.AtMgA` (see "Proved nearest to it"). They are not shown for a second, `AtMgR` (Johnson/P5J/AtMgR.lean:3-8; taken at Johnson/Desc/Kinds/Law/K06AtHead.lean:79, 104), whose start test stays a premise (that it passes is [evaluated] alone: Johnson/P5J/AtMgRAtHead.lean:10), nor for the other named conditions that a law takes beside the bundle (AtFullSide/Top/HeadMemberAtFull.lean:31-33).
- **Layer.** The protocol as written in Lean: the laws. The tie between Lean and the programs: whether their conditions hold at the stored sets.
- **Why it matters.** A law whose conditions do not hold at the stored sets says nothing there. That matters for (52).
- **Proved nearest to it.**
  - Each of the first chain's fourteen laws with content (thirteen kinds and the connecting rows: section 2.4, "Five counts") is a theorem about the very definition that the IOP calls, and Lean's kernel checks them. For the two further kinds, which have no place on that chain (the test on a committed table; the link's rounds), the proof calls further theorems: `k14_at_free_head`, `entrySound`, `keySound_at_live_holds`, `lastLeft_at_head_alone`. Each is reported to rest on Lean's three axioms when asked by name (the check: 18). Some bear "free" in their names: that refers to the values of the free parameters, at any of which the law is shown, not to a law without content.
  - No law of the theorem is about a definition that is called by nothing that runs, and no law has among its conditions the law's own statement, regrouped. Where the library holds statements of those forms, they are true and say nothing; no attack follows from them, and the theorem uses none.
  - For the bundle `P5J.AtMgA` (Johnson/P5J/MgAtAll.lean:31-37) there are values at which it holds: at all public files in scope that are read and at which the gate accepts (`P5J.head_of_live`, Johnson/P5J/HeadOfLive.lean:37-40). So, with no premise, it holds at the written copies of the two stored sets (`headMember_named`, AtFullSide/Top/HeadMemberAtGap.lean:70, in the side library).
  - AtFullSide/Top/HeadMemberAtGap.lean stands at the second hash function `OnBytes.secondHashFunction`, a fixed function, and at a constant in the digest's place, not at these files' digest. The closed facts beneath it have been checked by Lean's own kernel alone, and no independent checker has replayed them. It shows that this premise can be met; it adds nothing to what follows from the premise.

### 49 · The encoder's escape form at a chain's first hash call

- **The limit.** A chain's first hash call holds the digest of the public files only where the encoder writes the format's own string; with a proof parser other than the IOP's own, a first call can go without it. The same holds, at the proof file's first chain, of the request's item and file bytes as the format hashes them. A typed query that does not fit the format is written in an escape form: behind a byte that the format never hashes, spelled one to one, without the digest. The typed request itself is spelled, one to one, behind the escape.
  - With the IOP's own proof parser the block of a first call that carries the digest cannot take the escape form (the copy number is read as 64 bits, run values as 32 bytes). The other blocks of that typed query always stand behind the escape, spelled one to one.
  - For a proof parser other than the IOP's own together with public files chosen after challenges, which are outside the theorems (5), it is argued, not proved, that nothing is gained [paper].
  - Two unused functions that write no bit (`bkShout`, `eShout`) are in Johnson/PV/RunArgsFull.lean:66, 71, and nothing beneath `verify` depends on them. Were they used in the encoder, every escaped query would have one string, and a first hash call driven to the escape form would gain.
- **Layer.** The protocol as written in Lean.
- **Why it matters.** A first hash call behind the escape holds no digest of the public files, and (5) relies on that digest at the start of every chain.
- **Proved nearest to it.** The theorem for every proof parser (`sound_of_verify_parsers_at`, Johnson/Goal/Skeleton/SoundOfVerifyMore.lean:75; section 2.2) does range over proofs whose first calls lie behind the escape, and holds for them at its own error. It bounds the chance of an accept with a wrong output under the proof file's soundness at these files (`ProofSoundAt`; section 5, (1)), which is derived from the first chain's kinds' laws, not shown directly. So it has the same basis as the soundness theorem (28). The names between are covered by the audit of all declarations, with its one exception (18), and are not asked one by one.

### 61 · The copy's file and the decoder stand at written numbers

- **The limit.** The copy's file and the decoder stand at written numbers (`frPar`, OnBytes/Slice/FullRecSz.lean:55-63; `frS`, OnBytes/Slice/FullRecLink.lean:75-78). The numbers are a copy by hand of the lines of an example parameter file that the library contains as text (OnBytes/Slice/RecordFull.lean:11-13), and no theorem ties them to that text or to the reader of the parameter file (OnBytes/Slice/RecordFull.lean:33-37).

  The lists of rounds of the offline and the online IOP at the full size stand as written values too (`dryKeyFull`, Johnson/G/DryKeyLit.lean; `dryProofFull`, Johnson/G/DryProofLit.lean). `verify` computes these lists from the public files and compares them with the written ones, as a test of its own, and in scope, where they differ, it rejects every proof (Johnson/Desc/Laid/ProofAsks.lean:95-99; Johnson/Desc/Key/Asks.lean:62-69; Johnson/Desc/Ok/PlanWritten/Test.lean:101-120) [read: once, names followed in code].

  In scope, either `verify` rejects everything or both files' data are the written data (`written_or_no_all`, Johnson/Goal/Description/KeyWritten.lean:53). (The soundness proof itself uses the proof file's part of this, by `LPFD.Full.laidPOf_written`, Johnson/Desc/Laid/ProofFullScope.lean:38 [counted: a walk over constants; read].) At the written copy of the stored full-size set they are, by Lean's kernel, in the side library (`written_zFull`, AtFullSide/Top/WrittenAtFull.lean:34; 73). So a wrong number in a list would take nothing from soundness: the theorem is about this verifier, which would then reject everything at the true files. It would take all of completeness at the full size, and the side library's build would fail first [read]. What that does not settle: the lists are those of Lean's own planner, and that the C++ programs' planner makes the same rounds is run, not proved (LIMITS.md, L1); and in scope, away from the written copies, that this test passes is shown only as far as the theorems at those copies go (73): elsewhere `verify` may reject everything.
- **Layer.** The protocol as written in Lean: written numbers against Lean's own reader of the parameter file.
- **Why it matters.** For the theorem that is no gap: the numbers are part of `verify`'s own text. What they mean, by arithmetic done by hand [paper]:
  - the rate is 0.104;
  - the radius is 0.664 of the word's length: beyond half the distance, under the Johnson bound's 0.6775, so the committed table is one of a list, picked by the OOD sample;
  - 62 sampled columns give 97.6 bits, so each such round's 128 is about 97 bits and 32 of proof of work (36).
- **Proved nearest to it.** `recordTable_sizes` and `recordLink_recShape` (OnBytes/Slice/RecordPriceGlue.lean:236, 244) tie the sizes to what the planner derives at the typed lines `recordLines`. That is no tie to the example's text or to the reader.

### 63 · The key file: what no test asks

- **The limit.** That every sent hidden value stands in a claim is asked nowhere (Johnson/Desc/Key/Link.lean:33). A masked value that the committer sends and that no later claim uses is tested by nothing [read].
- **Layer.** The protocol as written in Lean.
- **Proved nearest to it.** `KeySound` (`keySound_holds`, Johnson/Goal/Conditions/KeyOpenHolds.lean:87, with no premise; section 5, (2)) is proved of the IOP in which the first of the key file's chains contains the links of Johnson/Desc/Key/Link.lean (one link for each lookup of the proof that the committed table's entries lie in their ranges) as they are, so it does not rest on the missing test (Johnson/Desc/Key/Link.lean:33-37). That it may be true and vacuous: (36).

### 79 · That the decision on the copy's file rejects a sampled position out of range is evaluated, not proved

- **The limit.** That the decision on the copy's file rejects a sampled position out of range is not proved in this repository. That a sampled position at or above the tree's size makes that decision reject is [evaluated: four views, at the small size] alone. Nor is anything proved of positions in range that are not the sampled ones. What is proved of that decision: (34).
- **Layer.** The protocol as written in Lean.
- **Tested nearest to it.** [evaluated] At the small size a form of the decision other than this one accepted four of 26,161 synthetic inputs: hand-made views in which a sampled position was raised out of its range, a malleable encoding of the verifier's own challenge, not a missed check on a prover's value. This decision, at the small size, rejected those four. Fourteen views with one position raised, given as input to two decisions, which the record does not name, gave 28 rejections of 28; one of the fourteen is surely out of range at the full size, the kind that was accepted at the small size. That no prover's bytes can produce such a view, and that `cp_verify` cannot form such an input from bytes, is [read]; no run bears on it. These runs cannot be repeated from this repository.

### 81 · What `scripts/check.py` does not check

- **The limit.** A pass of the full check (18) says nothing on the following. (b) to (e): [read: the script and its lists].
  - (a) *Lists made from the tree that is checked.* Some of the script's lists were made by a program from the very files that it checks: their verdicts cannot fail over these files. Nothing beneath the twenty-nine files that it pins is pinned (50). The soundness theorem is not among those for which the audit lists the definitions beneath the statement (scripts/listing-for.txt). Of five theorems (`VerifierAndGoals.filesRead`, `VerifierAndGoals.AtTheName.keySound_holds`, `OnBytes.WholeKey.wholeKeyPrivacy`, `LPFGuard.strike_verdict`, `VerifiableInference.Compilation.compiled_complete_atEveryCoin_holds`) the compiled statement is one name: the block in scripts/front-statements.txt pins that name alone, and what it says lies in the definition of that name, which that comparison does not reach; of the five definitions `FilesRead`'s alone stands in a pinned file (Ladder.lean; 50) [counted]. No false name is planted among the named theorems. Which lists, and which verdicts can fail: CHECK-RESULTS.md; LIMITS.md, L76.
  - (b) *Pages against lists.* No step compares the pages with the script's lists of theorems, so a page may name a theorem that has no line there and whose axioms the audit does not report by name. Of the theorems that a Lean file declares and this page names in code marks, at least 50 have none on scripts/top-theorems.txt [counted: by last name]. Seven theorems cannot have a line on scripts/top-theorems.txt, because the audit takes no named theorem whose statement rests on no declaration of the library: `Prob.ref_entry`, `Prob.ref_exponent`, `Compile.three_sum_range_le`, `TopC.Val2.level_pow`, `TopC.level_arith`, `TopC.ver_inv8` and `Code.Lines.coeff_swap`. That each exists is checked by a search of the source text. These seven are among the at least 50. A full check builds all of these, replays them by the kernel and covers them by the audit's verdicts on every declaration; one whole run of it over this repository's Lean files passed at every step [tested]; what its audit gave no verdict on, and what it does not cover: 18.
  - (c) *The command.* Of the nine files of the command `lean-verify` (45 (b)) the script checks nothing. The Lean files beneath the command are built and checked with the library, and one row of scripts/guards.txt contains the `#guard` commands of Johnson/PVR/ProgramGuard.lean. The script's rules on a program (guards, entry, reach, roots) name `verify-on-bytes` alone, from which this repository builds no stand-alone program (45).
  - (d) *Imports.* The step "imports" does not keep the goals' files apart from the proofs about them everywhere. Files of 50 folders of Johnson/ may import Ladder/, and a file of Ladder/ may import files of Johnson/Flat/, Johnson/PV/ and Johnson/Top/. Ladder/FitBinds.lean imports `Johnson.PV.FitO`, which reaches `Ladder.Ties`: there a file of Ladder/ depends on a file that is built on the goals, and no line of scripts/IMPORTS.txt bars it (50). Five files lie in a directory whose line does not name modules that they import, and a line for each permits these imports (Hiop/Seeded/Game.lean, OnBytes/Dry/ChainLaw.lean, OnBytes/Dry/SpecView.lean, Rom/Handle/Adv.lean, ZKCP/OnRange.lean); other files have such lines too; no file that a goal's statement unfolds to imports one of the five [counted].
  - (e) *Characters that look alike.* The script's table of characters that a Lean file may contain admits some that resemble others: curly quotation marks; bold, Fraktur, script and double-struck letters; 𝛍 and the micro sign µ beside μ; the Greek ο beside o; − beside the hyphen; and three combining accents, by which a letter such as ĝ can be spelt in two ways. For Lean a name spelt with one of them is a different name. No invisible, control or text-reordering character is admitted.
  - (f) *The HTML page of statements.* Its comparison with the Lean files (scripts/check_statements_html.py) is no step of the full check. It has been made over this repository's Lean files and the HTML page as it lies here; a second, longer page of statements, which the checker also reads and which is not in this repository, was made for that run from this repository's files by scripts/make_statements_html.py. The comparison ended with exit status 0, and all eight checks passed [tested].
  - (g) *The first steps of CHECKING.md.* They have been tried with a local mirror alone: `lake exe cache get` ended with exit status 0 and fetched none of Mathlib's compiled files; the packages' sources came without their git history, so their revisions were not compared with the manifest; and the build command was not run after it [tested]. On other machines that step, through a local mirror too, fetched every one of these files; why it ends both ways is not known (CHECKING.md, step 3). The public road (elan's installer, Lean's download, the public hosts) is untried. Mathlib and the other packages are taken as they were compiled beforehand, and the full check does not replay them. Two copies of them are in use; what follows on the two is known from reports alone. The 49,408 files of each with `.olean` in their names (the compiled files and Lake's checksum files beside them) are the same by checksum: one checksum was taken over each copy's files by the same rule, and the two are equal. The whole run of the full check told in 18's table used the copy whose Lean modules were, by three signs, compiled on the machine that made it; so did the build that [CHECK-RESULTS.md](CHECK-RESULTS.md) tells as made in seven calls; for the other runs that this page tells, which of the two they used is not traced. One copy's Lean modules this project compiled, and did not fetch compiled, by three signs on the machine that made it: a fetch of compiled files there by Mathlib's own tool brought none; each of the packages' 8,238 compiled modules, 7,878 of them Mathlib's, has a `.setup.json` file beside it, which, by a reading of Lake, Lake writes only beside what it compiles itself; and Mathlib's compiled files are dated in rising order over a quarter of an hour. The same holds of six of the other eight packages, whose compiled files are dated within a minute and a half, a span that ends while Mathlib's goes on. A seventh, proofwidgets, has the last two signs on its 12 modules, but its JavaScript files come from a public archive of compiled files fetched there. The eighth, Cli, has no compiled files. No log of that build is kept; the revisions that were asked for are the manifest's. The other copy's compiled files were, by its signs, fetched compiled with `lake exe cache get`: it has nine `.setup.json` files (six of Mathlib's package, three of batteries'), and its 8,240 `.olean` files (the 8,238 modules' and two `lakefile.olean`) are all dated within two minutes, which fits an unpacking and not a compiling; the command that made it is on no record. Neither copy's folders have git history, and the source files have not been compared with the manifest's revisions.
- **Layer.** The protocol as written in Lean: how the Lean proofs are checked.
- **Why it matters.** It affects no theorem. It bounds what "the full check passed" tells a reader: (b), (d) and (e) bear on reading statements by name and by eye, (c) on the one command that evaluates the executable form of `verify`.
- **Known attack or counterexample.** None known.

## 4. Theorems that are not about `theDescription`

Not every Lean file in this folder is about `theDescription` (section 2). Those on the two IOPs of Johnson/Flat/TwoSizes.lean are about two further renderings in Lean of the three IOPs at the default setting; they are known to differ from the programs; no page lists where. What is proved at the two IOPs: 13, 3. Their limits: [LIMITS.md](LIMITS.md). This section contains the gaps that bear on how these theorems may be read beside `verify`. Of its entries 39, 10 and 6 are about `verify` and its system, and 41 (a) is about the compiler that `verify` itself calls.

### 13, 3 · `innerCompiledVerifier_sound`, the theorem on `innerCompiledVerifier`, is a conditional theorem about renderings other than `theDescription`

The numbers name this one entry.

- **The limit.** `innerCompiledVerifier_sound` (Johnson/Top/InnerCompiledVerifier.lean:299) bounds the error of a compiled verifier from 16 named hypotheses. It is stated of parameters: in its own file no protocol is put in, and that file shows none that meets the 16 (Johnson/Top/InnerCompiledVerifier.lean:28-29). Both errors of its bound are free fields: at an error of 1 it says nothing. None of this affects the soundness of `verify`: the theorem of (28) has none of the 16 as a hypothesis.
  - (a) A further theorem ties the exit code that `verify-on-bytes` computes to `innerCompiledVerifier` under six more hypotheses that are not proved, and only at statements that a free field, `Served`, admits: where that field is false the theorem says nothing of `innerCompiledVerifier` (`exit_innerCompiledVerifier`, Johnson/Top/TieInnerCompiledVerifier.lean:240, :252; the field: Johnson/Top/TieInnerCompiledVerifier.lean:105). `Served`, `recOf` and the reading R are parameters (Johnson/Top/TieInnerCompiledVerifier.lean:69).
  - (b) All 16 with no hypothesis left hold at a toy alone (Johnson/Top/Toy/CompilerSoundnessConditionsAtSmallExample.lean:237). At the two IOPs of Johnson/Flat/TwoSizes.lean, at which this theorem is taken (TERMS.md), four of the 16 are left in general, `keySound`, `proofSound`, `commitment` and `sound` (`RemainingSoundnessConditionsFromDryRunAtFullSize`, Johnson/Flat/TwoSizes.lean:239; `RemainingSoundnessConditionsFromDryRunAtSmallSize`, Johnson/Flat/TwoSizes.lean:470), and MCA as well. Where more of the parameters is fixed, fewer are left, each time with MCA as a hypothesis: three at the full size, `keySound`, `proofSound` and `sound` (`innerCompiledVerifierSoundFromDryRunAtFullSize_withId`, Johnson/Flat/CommitCodeSound.lean:164) and, at the reference shape's numbers (`numsRef`), with lawful scales (`Lawful`) and the library's own functions that build an instance and give the next token (`Flat.buildReq`, `Flat.nextTok`), two: `keySound` and `proofSound` (`soundThreeFull_ref_withId`, Johnson/Flat/CommitCodeSound.lean:228).
  - (c) If the data of a file do not pass a guard, the soundness hypothesis that depends on that guard holds vacuously.
  - (d) The model's fixed tables stay, and its scales must be the files' own: at other ones an honest prover would refute a bound.
- **Layer.** The protocol as written in Lean, at IOPs other than `theDescription`, and, for `exit_innerCompiledVerifier`, the Lean text `verify-on-bytes`.
- **Why it matters.** Of `verify-on-bytes` there is only this conditional soundness. The proof of the theorem of (28) does not go through `innerCompiledVerifier_sound`: that name was not among the 204,124 constants that the theorem depended on [counted]. It goes through a form with digests as answers (28 (a)).
- **Proved nearest to it.** The state of the hypotheses at the IOPs other than `theDescription` at which the theorem is taken:

  | Hypotheses | State at those IOPs |
  |---|---|
  | `keySound`, `proofSound` | not proved there. In the files of section 4 nothing of `keySound` is proved at the default setting's numbers. At `theDescription`, by contrast, where the tests on the public files pass, `keySound` is a theorem (`keySound_holds`, Johnson/Goal/Conditions/KeyOpenHolds.lean:87), and so is `proofSound` at each set of public files in scope that is read (as typed, at every Z: `proofSound_of_verify`, Johnson/Goal/Skeleton/SoundOfVerifyGoals.lean:71, which follows from `proofSoundAt_of_verify`, Johnson/Goal/Skeleton/SoundOfVerifyMore.lean:69, because no public files are in scope unless they are read: `read_of_scope`, Johnson/Goal/Skeleton/SoundOfVerifyGoals.lean:60). Both theorems are typed at `dataAtFiles flashProveData Z`, the IOP from which `theDescription` is built, with two fixed values in the place of the verifier's two public readings, and name neither `verify` nor `theDescription` [read] (1, 2) |

### 39 · The six top statements as written, and their nearest forms

- **Proved: the six lines, and the three properties as an IOP.** Completeness and zero knowledge are proved in Lean for the protocol, at full size, each up to a small stated error: first for the interactive protocol (IOP), then for the compiled (non-interactive) prover and the same Lean verifier, in the random-oracle model. The honest prover in these theorems is a Lean definition, not the CUDA prover.

  Completeness covers well-formed requests on which the model has a result and the claimed output is that result. Where the model has no result it says nothing. Further theorems are about weights that pass the first worst-case check, or the least-bound check with numbers that hold (these are the library's own checks; neither is `cp_commit`'s worst-case check): at these the model has a result on every well-formed request. They cover no request that completeness after compiling does not already cover: that holds at all weights, for every well-formed request whose claimed output is the model's result. What this premise on the weights yields is said of every prompt, not of every request (the completeness theorem at weights that pass a worst-case check, below).

  Made-up weights are shown by name to pass the first check, and at the reference settings every set of weights whose layers lie within one stated bound is shown to pass the first check, or the least-bound check with numbers that hold (`passesSomeWorstCaseCheck_atReferenceSettings`); no theorem says whether a real model's weights do. Zero knowledge holds against an honest verifier in the IOP and, after compiling, against any verifier within a bound on its hash queries and replies, on one copy of the table; the simulator's running time is not modelled. Both are at the exact-match policy, with fresh random masks, for one commitment and one request per proof, not for batches. One bit per request is revealed (served or refused), and it depends on the weights. A service that chooses to say "failed" reveals one label of three in its place (served, failed or other tokens). Neither completeness nor zero knowledge says anything of what the public files themselves tell of the weights, or of timing.

  Each theorem named in the three tables below is part of this library's build (the record of that build: [CHECK-RESULTS.md](CHECK-RESULTS.md)) and rests on no axiom beyond Lean's three standard ones (`propext`, `Classical.choice`, `Quot.sound`) [tested]. Those whose file lies under AtFullSide/ are in the side library, which is built with a larger thread stack, and those on completeness and zero knowledge rest on a third, ShapeLines/, built with a still larger one: what goes with that is told at 73 (h) and LIMITS.md, L69. Of the six, the independent checker has accepted `sound`, `repliesFit` and `refusedHandsOutNoProof` by name, over a build of this repository's files; over such a build the three on binding, completeness and zero knowledge have been checked by Lean's kernel alone. Of the other theorems of the three tables no pass by name is told here, and the pass on the export of the soundness theorem did not reach them, since they do not lie beneath that theorem (18 (b)). How to check them: [CHECKING.md](CHECKING.md).
  - *The protocol and its honest provers.* At given public files the protocol is one Lean term, `AtPublicFiles.AtFiles.protocolAtFiles files` (E2E/BridgeJoinAtFiles.lean): an interactive oracle proof (IOP) in three parts. Offline: the proof in the key file, on the committed table. Link: from a further table to the first. Online: the proof for one request, on that further table. Every committed table has an out-of-domain sample, offline and online; the masking words that the provers send beside them have none, by design. `protocolAtFiles files` is the object in the input at which `verify` calls the compiled verifier (`object_description`, E2E/DescriptionTie.lean; E2E/CompleteAsIOPAtFiles.lean:54).

    The honest provers are `AtPublicFiles.AtFiles.HonestTerm.honestProvers files (thePartsAt files)` (E2E/TheHonestProver.lean, E2E/KeyArgumentsAt.lean). The theorems on completeness and zero knowledge take them as `VerifiableInference.Compilation.theProvers` (Johnson/Face/HonestProversWithCompiledPart.lean): the same provers, made to refuse a request that is not served. They are Lean definitions, not programs that run: among other things the model's run on a request is a specification, obtained by a non-constructive choice, and is not computed. Each theorem below is about these definitions exactly as written, default values included. None of them says anything of the released programs: the C++ verifier and committer and the CUDA prover.
  - *Their hypotheses.* Three tests on the public files: the files are in scope (`VerifierAndGoals.InScope files = true`: the full-size layout file, and the parameter file but for eight numbers, of which theta is at least 2^8; the scale file is free), they pass the gate (`protocolDataWellFormed (asDescriptionFiles files) = true`), and they are read without error (`readFiles files = .ok parameters`). The compiled lines ask exact match as well (`FilesInUse`, Johnson/Face/FinalStatement.lean). The library's stored full-size files, `VerifierAndGoals.storedPublicFiles` (Ladder/Stored.lean), meet all of these (`premises_zFull`, AtFullSide/Bind/LiveAtNamed.lean; `filesInUse_stored`, AtFullSide/CompletenessAndZeroKnowledge/LinesAtStoredFiles.lean). They are values written in Lean, tied by no theorem to a file on a disk (a script compares them: 73 (k)).
  - *The compiled system.* `compile (flashProve setup)`, that is `finalSystem honestProversAndSettings files settings`, where `setup` (`PublicSetup`) contains the three public files, what they read as, and that they are in use: the protocol above, compiled with a hash function, at the honest part `honestProversAndSettings` = `honestProversAndSettingsOf thePartsAt` (AtFullSide/Face/HonestProversAndSettingsValue.lean), one closed Lean term. Its committer and prover are `compile`'s own. Its verifier is `verify`, whatever the honest part (`compile_flashProve_verify_eq`). The hash function is drawn at random: these are statements in the random-oracle model.

  | The line | What it says | Name | File (of the theorem; where none is named, of the statement) | Status |
  |---|---|---|---|---|
  | `sound` | An output that is not that of the weights that an extractor reads off the commitment and the committer's queries is accepted with probability at most about q/2^124 + 2^-101, where q counts the committer's and the prover's hash queries. It holds at every honest part, and it is `Sound` of `verify`'s own system (`compile_flashProve_sound_iff`). | `VerifiableInference.sound` (more widely: `VerifiableInference.Compilation.finalSound`) | AtFullSide/Face/TheSixLines.lean (Johnson/Face/FinalSoundProof.lean) | proved |

Run by run: what an honest verifier sees of each run (offline, link, online) has exactly the distribution that the simulator makes from the public files, the verifier's messages, the out-of-domain samples, the columns of the tables that the verifier reads and, for a request, the request with its claimed output and the copy's number. - **The limits.** These go with every use of the tables above.
  - (a) The lines `completeOnCanServe`, `zeroKnowledgeOnCanServe` and `repliesFit`, and the corollaries of the first two, rest on the two statements on the IOP, completeness on served requests and honest-verifier zero knowledge (the table "As an IOP, before hashing"), through the compiler's theorems. They are worth what these two are worth: everything that goes with those goes with these. The step is made of calls alone.
  - (b) One request to a proof, one commitment, one copy of the table, the exact-match policy, and the Lean prover as a definition, default values included. The provers as written send default values at named places; no theorem says that a default value is never sent, and that none of them matters at public files in scope is not shown (E2E/KeyArgumentsAt.lean:19-24).
  - (c) The format's second hash function, a fixed public function inside the description, is no random oracle. At BLAKE2b no theorem shows that `verify` accepts anything: there it is tested by runs (52, 14).
  - (d) Where the model has no result, or another result than the claimed output, the line on completeness says nothing: at a real failure the prover returns the empty proof, the same as for a claimed output that is not the model's. That a request is refused is unchecked, as the label is: an empty proof proves nothing, and a prover can return it at a request that it could serve. A service that chooses to say "failed" says it unproved.

    As an IOP too, completeness speaks of the model's own output alone: of an output that the files' policy would accept and that is not the model's it says nothing. The requests of which the line `completeOnCanServe` speaks are those that meet `CanServe` and whose output is the model's (`finalComplete_iff_completeOn`, Johnson/Face/Checks.lean), so the limits of that condition go with it (LIMITS.md, L27).

    The completeness theorem at weights that pass a worst-case check does not change this: it does not make completeness hold where the model has no result; it names weights at which that case does not arise. No theorem says that the second disjunct holds of any weights of which the first does not: the premise contains the first check (whatever passes it meets the premise), and no theorem says that it is wider. With it go:

    - The premise's first disjunct is this library's own decidable test on settings, tables and weights.
    - Its second is a statement that numbers exist that hold together ("numbers that hold": each stretch bound that the least-bound check reads is at least 0, and no vector of whole numbers gets longer by more than that factor when it is multiplied with its matrix; which matrices, and what they are made from: `LeastBoundNumbers.Hold`, IntegerModel/Bounds/LeastBoundCheck.lean), and that with them a second test, the least-bound check, passes: a statement, not a test.
    - As it is written the first check can be evaluated at small sizes; at the full sizes it cannot in practice (the head of IntegerModel/Bounds/Check.lean).
    - The least-bound check is costly to evaluate (the head of IntegerModel/Bounds/LeastBoundCheck.lean).
    - To pass the least-bound check is not enough: at a small made-up model, with tables of its own, it passes with numbers that do not hold, at weights at which the model has no value on its one prompt (`control_leastBound_passes_with_zeros`, `control_leastBound_zeros_do_not_hold`, IntegerModel/Bounds/LeastBoundCheckControl.lean; `control_model_eq_none`, IntegerModel/Bounds/LengthCheckControl.lean).
    - Neither check is `cp_commit`'s worst-case check (LIMITS.md, L7): no theorem relates either to a check that any program makes.
    - Both checks are sufficient, not necessary: weights that do not pass may still never fail (the heads of IntegerModel/Bounds/Check.lean and IntegerModel/Bounds/LeastBoundCheck.lean); no theorem shows such weights.
    - No theorem says whether a real model's weights meet the premise.
    - Nothing in a commitment, in a proof or in what a verifier does shows that the committed weights meet the premise.
    - The weights that are shown by name to pass a check that gives it are made up (in full: LIMITS.md, L93).

    Whether a request is served depends on the weights. At some valid weights no request meets `CanServe` (`canServe_nowhere_atHaltingWeights`, VerifiableInference/ModelGuardsAtReference.lean:225), and there `completeAtWellFormedRequests` says nothing. That some request is served is said by a lemma beside it, at made-up weights (`someRequestIsServed_holds`, VerifiableInference/Final/ZeroKnowledgeAtTheProtocolLaws.lean). No request is shown to be served at a real model's weights.
  - (e) Before hashing, one bit for each request is not hidden: whether the request is served, which depends on the weights. After compiling, one bit for each request is revealed, not hidden: served or refused. It depends on the weights, and over many requests the bits tell what the model answers (in full: LIMITS.md, L93). A true output is refused if the model has no value further on in the window (the 1,024 tokens that the model's run reads: the prompt, the claimed output, then zeros), so the bit tells more than what the model answers. In the corollary "failed" can come from these zeros too.

    The simulator may depend on the public files, and it answers the hash queries in the hash function's place. The line says nothing of what the public files themselves tell of the weights, of what is published beside the commitment, or of timing. Nor does it say anything about a party that learns the committer's randomness or the weights by other means than the proofs. Beyond 688,127 replies or 2^64 hash queries it says nothing.

    A nonce search that runs out is inside what is simulated: it shows as the empty proof at a served request, which looks like the reply to a refused request; in the corollary, as the empty proof beside the label "served". The simulators' running time is not modelled. The verifier has no hash query before the commitment, and the weights and the public files are fixed before the hash function is drawn. At weights at which no request is served the line speaks of the commitment alone: every reply is the empty proof (in the corollary, of the commitment and the labels).
  - (f) The compiled prover keeps no state: it draws its copy at random (at this honest part there is one), nothing counts the replies, and all its coins are fresh and uniform; nothing is said for coins derived from a short key, as the programs derive theirs from a secret seed (15 (3)), or for batches.
  - (g) A statement of the form of `ZeroKnowledgeOnServedRequests` can hold trivially: a prover that sent fixed messages, or that always stopped for a public reason, would meet it. What excludes that is completeness of the same provers, so the two are to be read together, as about one triple of provers and one protocol. The like holds after compiling: the line `zeroKnowledgeOnCanServe` counts only beside the line `completeOnCanServe` of the same system, and so do their corollaries.
  - (h) In the run-by-run form, at all three runs, the simulator is handed two things that are made from the weights, the out-of-domain samples and the columns read: so that form alone does not speak of the weights. The clause on the whole life does: there the samples and the columns are those of tables of the other weights. It holds within a budget of 44,040,191 columns read of each table, and the numbers above are the largest that fit it.
  - (i) The numbers are over different distributions and are not to be compared. 2^-96 a round and 2^-79 a run are over challenges whose proof-of-work bits are 0. 2^-41 is before hashing, over the provers' coins and the challenges that pass the proof-of-work tests. 2^-40 is after hashing, over the hash function and the coins, the searches for a nonce that run out included. 2^-174 bounds the private stops of one online run; it is not the error of a commitment's life, which is the 2^-135, a statistical distance before hashing; that figure is not the 2^-135 of the appendix's head, another statement's figure (fixed-message provers, one copy, one request, after hashing). 2^-154 is a statistical distance at 2^64 hash queries and 688,127 replies, one copy, after hashing. None is a security level, and no figure here is 128 bits.
  - (j) No running time is claimed for the extractor, which is a function and is not shown computable, and nothing but the weights is extracted. The committer is a Lean term that no theorem ties to a program; the commitment has 256 bits, and the key file is not under it. The lines on soundness and binding read no prover: soundness is the same at every honest part, binding at every one whose tables' salts have one length, the same whatever the public readings return.
  - (k) `CompleteIOP` and `HonestVerifierZeroKnowledge` are this library's own definitions of completeness of an IOP with proof of work and of honest-verifier zero knowledge; `ZeroKnowledgeBelowWhere` and `ZeroKnowledgeShownBelowWhere` too are this library's own forms. That they say what these words say is a reading, not a theorem.
  - (l) Nothing in `ThreeAtFiles` ties the encoder, which completeness and zero knowledge read, to the decoder, which soundness reads. Its field on zero knowledge is the first part of that statement, `HonestVerifierZeroKnowledge` (run by run, and the whole view at every budget that fits the tables). The clause on the whole life at 710,324 copies and 688,127 requests, with its 2^-135, and the two guards (the fields `someRequestIsServed` and `twoIndicesServeOneRequest`: some request is served, and two different indices serve one request) are the theorem's on zero knowledge, not this structure's.
  - (m) One name is declared in two namespaces: `AtPublicFiles.AtFiles.HonestTerm.CompleteAtFiles` is the statement on the IOP, and `VerifiableInference.Compilation.CompleteAtFiles` is the compiled line. No line bounds the length of a proof, or the hash queries that `verify` itself makes (LIMITS.md, L93).
- **The limit: the six top statements as written.** As the goals `PackageSound` to `PackageBundle` ask them (Ladder/Package.lean:92-191; that is "as written" here), the six top statements (`Sound`, `SoundAndBinding`, `SoundOpened`, `Complete`, `ZeroKnowledge`, `CompleteSoundZeroKnowledge`: VerifiableInference/Statements.lean:84, :109, :142, :164, :308, :332) range over all files in scope, tolerant policies too, and over all requests, the empty one too. So all six are known or expected to fail at the goals' family of files (Ladder/Package.lean:40-43, :177-178, :189-190). This is a fault of the statements as written or, for the three on soundness, of the family of files at which they are asked, not of the protocol, and it does not affect the soundness of `verify` at exact match (28). In their place the library's goals ask nearest forms; for `ZeroKnowledge` and for the three together none is stated, and for `Complete` only G3'pol, under a policy ((d), (e), (f)).
  - (a) Of the nearest forms, which are asked of the Lean verifier's own system, one is proved, for `Sound` alone (below).
  - (b) The two nearest forms that speak of openings, `NearestSoundAndBinding` and `NearestSoundOpened` (Ladder/Package.lean:123, :145), are asked of the Lean verifier's own system, and are proved only of an illustrative system (55). `SoundAndBinding` is proved, beside that, of the compiled system at the honest part, at public files in use (55 (b)): the line `soundAndBinding` (above). Of the Lean verifier's system `NearestSoundAndBinding` is false [paper] (55 (a)); `NearestSoundOpened` is not proved of it and could hold only vacuously, since, but for the soundness error, `verify` accepts nothing under the constant committer's commitment (`hollow_meets_join_never`, Johnson/Bind/Cpp/JoinControls.lean:127-135). Of `cp_commit` nothing is stated.
  - (c) The statements without the empty output (VerifiableInference/StatementsNonEmpty.lean) are asked by no goal.
  - (d) For `Complete` and for `ZeroKnowledge` no nearest form is stated as a goal in Ladder/Package.lean. The one goal near `Complete`, G3'pol, stands in Ladder.lean and is under a policy ((e)). What is proved on completeness is the line `completeOnCanServe` (above), at the requests that can be served and whose output is the model's. What is proved on zero knowledge is the line `zeroKnowledgeOnCanServe` with its corollary (above), and there whether a request is served is not hidden ("The limits", (e)).
  - (e) One goal names `CanServe`: the goal on completeness under a policy (G3'pol, Ladder.lean:489) [counted]. It is asked of the system built from a description, whose honest side is a constant one (55), and is not proved [counted]. The six lines are of the compiled system at the honest part, and no theorem makes the two one system.

    The line's two forms on completeness are tied: the line `completeOnCanServe` is `CompleteOn` on `CanServe`, of the compiled system; `completeAtWellFormedRequests` is equivalent to it (`finalComplete_iff_completeOn`, Johnson/Face/Checks.lean). For zero knowledge the line `zeroKnowledgeOnCanServe` is `ZeroKnowledgeOn` on `CanServe`, for an error that stays at or below 2^-154 within the limits, of the compiled system at the Lean honest part, at all public files in use, with no premise left; its corollary `zeroKnowledgeAtEveryRequest` is in another form, `ZeroKnowledgeBelowWhere` (LIMITS.md, L18).
  - (f) `CompleteSoundZeroKnowledge` (VerifiableInference/Statements.lean:332) is a bare conjunction: nothing bounds the verifier's or the honest side's work, so a useless system meets all three together [paper]. It is proved of no system [counted]. No nearest form of it is stated as a goal.
