## 6. Checks of which no run over these files is told here, the state of the files, and what the theorems of 39 do not reach

What is stated here is said of the Lean files as they lie in this repository. What has been run over them, and how the build was made: [CHECK-RESULTS.md](CHECK-RESULTS.md). One whole run of the full check over them, on one machine, is told here, and passed at every step (18) [tested]; what it does not cover: 18, 81.

**Checks of which no run over these files is told here, or a run of a part alone.**

- No pass of the independent checker over the whole of a build of this repository's files is told here; passes on single theorems' exports are (18 (b)).
- Of the walk that takes checksums over the text of `verify`, by which runs over other states of the files are tied to one another (26; it is another walk than the one over the constants at 73 (h)), no run over this repository's files is told here.
- Of the counts and walks told at 56, but for the two checks that every build makes (`#guard_cone`, `#guard_has`), no repeat over this repository's files is told here.
- Of the names in the tables of 39, those of the theorems of AtFullSide/Face/TheSixLines.lean, the six lines among them, `modelNeverFails_of_passesSomeWorstCaseCheck` and the four theorems of AtFullSide/Face/ExecutableVerifierIsLeanVerifier.lean have a stored statement in scripts/front-statements.txt (four of its 188 stored statements are those of the file that was added afterwards: "Runs and counts"; the other 184 are Lean's own print over a build of Lean files that differ from this repository's nowhere but in comments, in docstrings and in the text of 43 strings; none of the 184 was stored from a listing over a build of this repository's own files; the full check's run over these files found the compiled statements of these 184 to be the stored ones: 18): the audit looks the others up by name alone (LIMITS.md, L24).
- Of the files that `scripts/check.py` pins, some have a checksum that is taken from the repository's own bytes: such a pin cannot fail over the repository as it is (LIMITS.md, L76).
- The script's table of characters that a Lean file may contain (81 (e)) admits, among many others, these fifteen: twelve letters that files declare as notation, ★, ᵉ and υ.

**The files.**

- A comment in a Lean file may say more or less than the statement beneath it. The comment does not govern: the statement counts (LIMITS.md, L23; README.md). One that is out of date: AtFullSide/Top/LiveAtFull.lean:28 says that nothing in the main library imports that file; E2E/KeyStartsBare.lean:50 does [read].
- Some names say more than the theorem proves, some are declared in more than one namespace (one of them: 39, "The limits", (m)), and some are short. Seven definitions of propositions are plain `True` (`k14On`, `k14Bud`, Johnson/Desc/ProofFile/Honest/K14/PartsCalls.lean:67, :70; `k13On`, `k13Bud`, Johnson/Desc/ProofFile/Honest/K13/RowsLot.lean:53, :56; `Any`, Johnson/Desc/First/Att/Claims/RS.lean:42 and Johnson/Desc/ProofFile/Honest/K2/Law/Alike.lean:52; `Clean`, Johnson/Top/H/FlPort/Kit.lean:376) [counted: lines that end in ": Prop := True"]; by a walk over the constants none of them lies in what the statement of one of the six lines, or of `sound_of_verify_at`, rests on: they stand beneath proofs alone [counted]. Two definitions are named `True` inside namespaces of their own and are not Lean's `True` (Blocks/Form.lean:165; Blocks/ClosingBlock.lean:115) [read].
- The list of rounds is written as a base list with one entry replaced (`Fmt.M3.roundsOf` and the declarations related to it), not written out.
- The limits of the line `soundAndBinding` are told in full in the head of Johnson/Face/FinalBindingStatement.lean; the chief of them stand in LIMITS.md, L93, and THEOREM.md, and a part on this page (39: the line's row, and "The limits", (j)).
- Could the theorem on zero knowledge hold for a trivial reason? The main guard is completeness of the same Lean provers, at the same public files and the same served requests (39, "The limits", (g)). The provers' messages are split into kinds of sub-protocol, of which thirteen have lists of messages. No lemma that this page names says, kind by kind, that the stated number of lists is in the prover's table, nor that a run calls any part of a list.

**What the theorems of 39 do not reach.** (All but the second at more length, and further limits: 39, its opening and "The limits".)

- Completeness says nothing where the model has no result. The completeness theorem at weights that pass a worst-case check does not change this: it does not make completeness hold where the model has no result; it names weights at which that case does not arise (39: the table beside the six lines, and "The limits", (d)).
- That every sampled oracle message of the protocol at public files in scope has an out-of-domain answer and a radius within the Johnson condition is proved at the library's written full-size list of such messages (`every_message_is_within_johnson_and_at_or_beyond_half`, Johnson/G/PackLitArms.lean) and, at all public files in scope, of the committed table (`table_in_scope`, `sample_and_johnson_in_scope`, E2E/SampleAndJohnsonInScope.lean); no theorem says it at the protocol's own term. ("out-of-domain": "Terms used on this page", OOD sample.)
- One request to a proof; one commitment; exact match. Nothing on batches.
- Zero knowledge after compiling: one copy of the table, up to 688,127 replies and 2^64 hash queries, and beyond these nothing. One bit per request is revealed (served or refused), and it depends on the weights. A service that chooses to say "failed" reveals one label of three in its place (served, failed or other tokens).
- All the committer's and the prover's coins are fresh and uniform; the programs derive theirs from a secret seed.
- Nothing of what the public files themselves tell of the weights, of what is published beside the commitment, or of timing. The simulators' running time is not modelled.
- Nothing is proved about the released programs: the C++ verifier and committer and the CUDA prover.
- The definitions of completeness and of zero knowledge, at the level of the IOP and after compiling, are this library's own.
- The honest prover is a definition, not a program: it obtains the model's trace by a non-constructive choice.
